Paradifferential quantization.

Given
p\in{\mathbb{N}},
we consider functions

\chi_{p}\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{p}\times{\mathbb{R}};{\mathbb{R}})
and
\chi\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}};{\mathbb{R}})
,
even with respect to each of their arguments, satisfying, for
0<\delta_{0}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{10}
,

\displaystyle{\rm{\displaystyle supp}}\,\chi_{p}\subset\{(\xi^{\prime},\xi)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}\times{\mathbb{R}}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad|\xi^{\prime}|\leqslant\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle\},\qquad\chi_{p}(\xi^{\prime},\xi)\equiv 1\,\,\,\rm{for}\,\,\,|\xi^{\prime}|\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle,

(3.8)

\displaystyle{\rm\displaystyle supp}\,\chi\subset\{(\xi^{\prime},\xi)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad|\xi^{\prime}|\leqslant\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle\},\qquad\quad\chi(\xi^{\prime},\xi)\equiv 1\,\,\,\rm{for}\,\,\,|\xi^{\prime}|\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\delta_{0}\langle\xi\rangle.

For
p=0,
we set
\chi_{0}\equiv 1
.
We assume moreover that

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\partial_{\xi^{\prime}}^{\beta}\chi_{p}(\xi^{\prime},\xi)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ell,\beta}\langle\xi\rangle^{-\ell-|\beta|},\quad\forall\ell\in{\mathbb{N}},\,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}^{p},

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\partial_{\xi^{\prime}}^{\beta}\chi(\xi^{\prime},\xi)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{\ell,\beta}\langle\xi\rangle^{-\ell-\beta},\quad\forall\ell,\,\beta\in{\mathbb{N}}.

If
a(x,\xi)
is a smooth symbol
we define its Weyl quantization as the operator
acting on a

2\pi
-periodic function

u

as

{\rm Op}^{W}(a)u(x)=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\Big(\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\hat{a}\big(k-j,\tfrac{k+j}{2}\big)u_{j}\Big){e^{{\rm i}kx}},

where
\hat{a}(k,\xi)
is the
k^{th}-
Fourier coefficient of the
2\pi-
periodic function
x\mapsto a(x,\xi)
.

Definition 3.4
.

(Bony-Weyl quantization)

If
a(U;x,\xi)
is a symbol in
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{m}_{p}
,
respectively in
\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0}]
,
we set

\displaystyle a_{\chi_{p}}(U;x,\xi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{\jmath}\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{*})^{p}\\
\vec{\sigma}\in\{\pm\}^{p}\end{subarray}}\chi_{p}(\vec{\jmath},\xi)a_{\vec{\jmath}}^{\vec{\sigma}}(\xi)u_{\vec{\jmath}}^{\vec{\sigma}}e^{{\rm i}(\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\jmath})x},

\displaystyle a_{\chi}(U;x,\xi)

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}}\chi(j,\xi)\hat{a}(U;j,\xi)e^{{\rm i}jx},

where in the last equality
\hat{a}(U;j,\xi)
stands for
j^{th}
Fourier coefficient of
a(U;x,\xi)
with respect to the
x
variable, and
we define the
Bony-Weyl
quantization of
a(U;\cdot)
as

\displaystyle{\OpBWPlain\left(a({U};\cdot)\right)}v(x)\triangleq{\rm Op}^{W}(a_{\chi_{p}}({U};\cdot))v(x)=\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\vec{\jmath},j,k)\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{*})^{p+2}\\
\vec{\sigma}\in\{\pm\}^{p}\\
\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\jmath}+j=k\end{subarray}}\chi_{p}\left(\vec{\jmath},\frac{j+k}{2}\right)a_{\vec{\jmath}}^{\vec{\sigma}}\left(\frac{j+k}{2}\right)u_{\vec{\jmath}}^{\vec{\sigma}}v_{j}{e^{{\rm i}kx}}\,,

(3.9)

\displaystyle{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U;\cdot)\right)}v(x)\triangleq{\rm Op}^{W}(a_{\chi}(U;\cdot))v(x)=\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{(j,k)\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{*})^{2}}\!\!\!\chi\left(k-j,\frac{j+k}{2}\right)\hat{a}\left(U;k-j,\frac{k+j}{2}\right)v_{j}{e^{{\rm i}kx}}\,.

(3.10)

Note that if

\chi\Big(k-j,\tfrac{k+j}{2}\Big)\neq 0

then
|k-j|\leqslant\delta_{0}\langle\frac{j+k}{2}\rangle

and therefore, for
\delta_{0}\in(0,1)
,

\frac{1-\delta_{0}}{1+\delta_{0}}|k|\leqslant|j|\leqslant\frac{1+\delta_{0}}{1-\delta_{0}}|k|\,,\quad\forall j,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,.

This relation shows that the action of a paradifferential operator does not spread much the Fourier support of functions.

If
a
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]
,
we define its
Bony-Weyl
quantization

{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U;\cdot)\right)}=\sum_{q=p}^{N}{\OpBWPlain\left(a_{q}(U;\cdot)\right)}+{\OpBWPlain\left(a_{>N}(U;\cdot)\right)}.

We define as well

\displaystyle\OpvecPlain\left(a\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)\triangleq{\OpBWPlain\left(\begin{bmatrix}a\left(U;x,\xi\right)&0\\
0&\overline{a^{\vee}\left(U;x,\xi\right)}\end{bmatrix}\right)},

\displaystyle a^{\vee}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq a\left(U;x,-\xi\right).

(3.11)

Remark 3.5
.

•

The operator

{\OpBWPlain\left(a\right)}

maps functions with zero average in functions with zero average,
and
\Pi_{0}^{\bot}{\OpBWPlain\left(a\right)}={\OpBWPlain\left(a\right)}\Pi_{0}^{\bot}
.

•

If
a
is a homogeneous symbol,
the two definitions of quantization in (
3.9
)-(
3.10
) differ
by a smoothing operator according to
Definition
3.11
below, see
[
20
, page 50]
.

•

Definition
3.4

is independent of the cut-off functions
\chi_{p}
,
\chi
,
up to smoothing operators (Definition
3.11
).

•

The action of

{\OpBWPlain\left(a\right)}
on the spaces
\dot{H}^{s}
only depends
on the values of the symbol
a(u;t,x,\xi)

for
|\xi|\geqslant 1
.
Therefore, we may identify two symbols
a(u;t,x,\xi)
and

b(u;t,x,\xi)
if they agree for
|\xi|\geqslant 1/2
.
In particular, whenever we encounter a symbol that is not smooth at
\xi=0
,
such as, for example,
a=g(x)|\xi|^{m}
for
m\in{\mathbb{R}}^{*}
, or
\sgn{\xi}
,
we will consider its smoothed out version

\left(1-\chi(\xi)\right)a\left(x,\xi\right)
, where
\chi
is defined in (
3.8
). Similarly for
p
-homogeneous symbols.

Remark 3.6
.

Given a paradifferential operator

A={\OpBWPlain\left(a(x,\xi)\right)}
it results

\overline{A}={\OpBWPlain\left(\overline{a(x,-\xi)}\right)},\qquad A^{\intercal}={\OpBWPlain\left(a(x,-\xi)\right)},\qquad A^{*}={\OpBWPlain\left(\overline{a(x,\xi)}\right)},

where
A^{\intercal}
is the transposed operator with respect to the real scalar product

\left\langle\cdot\ \middle|\ \cdot\right\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}
in (
3.2
), and

A^{*}
denotes the adjoint operator with respect to the complex
scalar product
\left\langle\cdot\ \middle|\ \cdot\right\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}
on
\dot{L}^{2}
in (
3.2
). It results
A^{*}=\overline{A}^{\intercal}
.

•

A paradifferential operator
A={\OpBWPlain\left(a(x,\xi)\right)}
is
real
(i.e.
A=\overline{A}
) if

\overline{a(x,\xi)}=a(x,-\xi)\,.

(3.12)

•

It is
symmetric
(i.e.
A=A^{\intercal}
)
if

a(x,\xi)=a(x,-\xi).

We now provide the action of a paradifferential operator on Sobolev spaces, cf.
[
20
, Prop. 3.8]
.

Lemma 3.7

(Action of a paradifferential operator)
.

Let
m\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

i)

If
p\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there is
s_{0}>0
such that for any symbol
a
in
{\tilde{\Gamma}^{m}_{p}}
,
there is a constant
C>0
, depending only on
s
and on (
3.5
)
with
{\mathtt{b}}=\beta=0
,
such that, for any
(U_{1},\ldots,U_{p})
, for
p\geqslant 1
,

\left\|{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U_{1},\ldots,U_{p};\cdot)\right)}u_{p+1}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{s-m}}\leqslant C\left\|U_{1}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\cdots\left\|U_{p}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{s_{0}}}\left\|u_{p+1}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}.

If
p=0

the above bound holds replacing the right hand side with

C\left\|u_{p+1}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}
.

ii)

Let
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
p\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
K^{\prime}\leqslant K\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
a
in
{{\Gamma}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0}]}
.
There is
s_{0}>0
,
and a constant
C
, depending only on
s
,
\epsilon_{0}
, and on (
3.6
) with
0\leqslant\alpha\leqslant 2,\beta=0
,
such that, for any
t
in
I
, any
0\leqslant k\leqslant K-K^{\prime}
, any
U
in
{B^{K}_{{s_{0}}}(I;\epsilon_{0})}
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\OpBWPlain\left(\partial_{t}^{k}a(U;t,\cdot)\right)}\right\|_{{\mathcal{L}}\left(\dot{H}^{s},\dot{H}^{s-m}\right)}\leqslant C\|U(t,\cdot)\|_{k+K^{\prime},s_{0}}^{p}\,,

(3.13)

so that

\left\|{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U;t,\cdot)\right)}v(t)\right\|_{K-K^{\prime},s-m}\leqslant C\|U(t,\cdot)\|_{K,s_{0}}^{p}\|v(t)\|_{K-K^{\prime},s}
.

Classes of
m
-Operators and smoothing Operators.

Given integers
\left(n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+1}\right)\in({\mathbb{N}}^{*})^{p+1}
we denote by
\max_{2}\left\{n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+1}\right\}

the second largest among
n_{1},\ldots,n_{p+1}
.
We now define
m
-operators which include the class of paradifferential operators of order
m

(see Remark
3.12
) and allow to define the smoothing remainders when
the order
m
is negative (see Definition
3.11
).
The class
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{p}
denotes multilinear
operators that lose
m
derivatives
and are
p
-homogeneous in
u
,
while the class
\mathcal{M}_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}
contains non-homogeneous
operators which lose
m
derivatives,
vanish at degree at least
p
in
u
, satisfy tame estimates
and are
(K-K^{\prime})
-times differentiable in
t
.
The constant
\mu
in (
3.15
) takes into account possible loss of derivatives in the “low" frequencies.

The following definition is taken from
[
27
, Def. 2.5]
(see also its Fourier characterization in
[
27
, Lemma 2.9]
.

Definition 3.8

( Classes of
m
-operators)
.

Let
m\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
p,N\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

K,K^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
K^{\prime}\leqslant K
, and
\epsilon_{0}>0
.

i)

p
-homogeneous
m
-operators.

We denote by
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{p}

the space of
(p+1)
-linear symmetric translation invariant operators from

\left(\dot{H}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}^{2}\right)\right)^{p}\times\dot{H}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}\right)
to

\dot{H}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}\right)
, whose associated polynomial has the form

M(U)v\triangleq M\left(U,\ldots,U\right)v=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{\sigma}\in\{\pm\}^{p}\\
k-j=\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\jmath}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!M_{\vec{\jmath},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}}\,u_{\vec{\jmath}}^{\vec{\sigma}}\,v_{j}\,{e^{{\rm i}kx}}

(3.14)

with coefficients
M_{\vec{\jmath},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}}
symmetric in
(j_{1},\sigma_{1}),\ldots,(j_{p},\sigma_{p})
,
satisfying the following: there are
\mu\geqslant 0
,
C>0
such that, for any

\vec{\jmath}=(j_{1},\ldots,j_{p})\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{*})^{p}
,
j,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}
, it results

\left|M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}_{p}}\right|\leqslant C\ {\rm max}_{2}\left\{\left|j_{1}\right|,\ldots,\left|j_{p}\right|,\left|j\right|\right\}^{\mu}\ \max\left\{\left|j_{1}\right|,\ldots,\left|j_{p}\right|,\left|j\right|\right\}^{m},

(3.15)

If
p=0
the right hand side of (
3.14
) must be substituted with
\sum_{j\in{\bf Z}}M_{j}v_{j}e^{{\rm i}jx}
with
\left|M_{j}\right|\leqslant C\left|j\right|^{m}
.

ii)

Non-homogeneous
m
-operators.

We denote by
\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0}]

the space of operators
(U,t,v)\mapsto M(U;t)v
defined on
B^{K^{\prime}}_{s_{0}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
for some
s_{0}>0
,
which are linear in the variable
v
and such that the following holds true.
For any
s\geqslant s_{0}
there are
C>0
and

\epsilon_{0}(s)\in]0,\epsilon_{0}[
such that for any

U\in B^{K^{\prime}}_{s_{0}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)\cap C^{K}_{*}\left(I,\dot{H}^{s}({\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{C}})\right)
,
any
v\in C^{K-K^{\prime}}_{*}\left(I,\dot{H}^{s}({\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{C}})\right)
, any
0\leqslant k\leqslant K-K^{\prime}
,
t\in I
, we have that

\left\|{\partial_{t}^{k}\left(M(U;t)v\right)}\right\|_{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m}\leqslant C\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\left(\|{v}\|_{k^{\prime\prime},s}\|{U}\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},{s_{0}}}^{p}+\|{v}\|_{k^{\prime\prime},{s_{0}}}\|U\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},{s_{0}}}^{p-1}\|U\|_{k^{\prime}+K^{\prime},s}\right).

(3.16)

In case
p=0
we require the estimate

\|{\partial_{t}^{k}\left(M(U;t)v\right)}\|_{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m}\leqslant C\|v\|_{k,s}
.
We say that a non-homogeneous
m
-operator
M\left(U;t\right)
is
real
if it is real valued for any

u\in B^{K^{\prime}}_{s_{0}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
.

iii)

m
-Operators.

We denote by
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]

the space of operators

M(U;t)v=\sum_{q=p}^{N}M_{q}(U)v+M_{>N}(U;t)v,

(3.17)

where
M_{q}
are homogeneous
m
-operators in
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{q}
,
q=p,\ldots,N
and

M_{>N}
is a non–homogeneous
m
-operator
in
\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[\epsilon_{0}]
. We say that a
m
-operator
M\left(u;t\right)
is
real
if it is real valued for any

u\in B^{K^{\prime}}_{s_{0}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
.
We shall also denote with
\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m}_{q}
the subspace of

\Sigma{\mathcal{M}}_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}[\epsilon_{0},N]
made of pluri-homogeneous
m
-operators, namely symbols which expand as in (
3.7
) with
M_{>N}\equiv 0
.

Remark 3.9
.

By
[
27
, Lemma 2.8]
,
if
M(U_{1},\dots,U_{p})
is a
p
–homogeneous

m
-operator in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}
then

M(U)=M(U,\ldots,U)
is a non-homogeneous
m
-operator in

{\cal M}^{m}_{K,0,p}[\epsilon_{0}]
for any
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
K\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
We shall say that
M(u)
is in
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}
.

Notation 3.10
.

•

If
M(U_{1},\ldots,U_{p})
is a
p
-homogeneous
m
-operator, we shall often denote by
M(U)
the associated
p
-homogeneous polynomial, as in (
3.14
), and write
M(U)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}
. Conversely, a
p
-homogeneous polynomial can be represented by a
(p+1)
-linear form of the type
M(U_{1},\ldots,U_{p})U_{p+1}
, which may not be symmetric in the first
p
variables. If this form satisfies the symmetric estimate (
3.15
), then it corresponds to an
m
-operator in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}
obtained by symmetrizing the internal variables. In the sequel, we adopt this identification without further comment.

•

given an operator
M(U;t)
in
\Sigma{\mathcal{M}}_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}[r,N]
of the form (
3.17
)
we denote by

{\mathcal{P}}_{\leqslant N}[M(U;t)]\triangleq\sum_{q=p}^{N}M_{q}(U)\,,\quad\text{resp.}\qquad{\mathcal{P}}_{q}[M(U;t)]\triangleq M_{q}(U)\,,

(3.18)

the projections on the pluri-homogeneous, resp. homogeneous, operators in

\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{q}
, resp. in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{q}^{m}
. Given an integer
p\leqslant p^{\prime}\leqslant N
we also denote

{\mathcal{P}}_{\geqslant p^{\prime}}[M(U;t)]\triangleq\sum_{q=p^{\prime}}^{N}M_{q}(U),\qquad{\mathcal{P}}_{\leqslant p^{\prime}}[M(U;t)]\triangleq\sum_{q=p}^{p^{\prime}}M_{q}(U)\,.

The same notation will be also used to denote
pluri-homogeneous/homogeneous components of symbols.

If
m\leqslant 0
the operators in
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]
are referred to as smoothing operators.

Definition 3.11

(Smoothing operators)
.

Let
\varrho\geqslant 0
. A
(-\varrho)
-operator
R(U)
belonging to
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]

is called a smoothing operator.
We also denote

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\triangleq\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{-\varrho}_{p}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0}]\triangleq\mathcal{M}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0}]\,,

\displaystyle\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]\triangleq\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]\,,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{q}\triangleq\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{-\varrho}_{q}.

Remark 3.12
.

\bullet

Lemma
3.7
implies that, if
a(U;t,\cdot)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,

m\in\mathbb{R}
, then the associated paradifferential operator

{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U;t,\cdot)\right)}
defines a
m
-operator in

\Sigma{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

\bullet

The composition of smoothing operators
R_{1}\in{{\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p_{1}}[\epsilon_{0},N]}

and
R_{2}\in{{\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p_{2}}[\epsilon_{0},N]}
is a smoothing operator
R_{1}R_{2}
in

{{\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p_{1}+p_{2}}[\epsilon_{0},N]}
. This is a particular case of Proposition
3.17
-(
i)
below.

Definition 3.13

(
Homogeneous vector fields).

Let
m\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
p,N\in{\mathbb{N}}
. We denote by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{p+1}^{m}
the space of
(p+1)
-homogeneous vector fields of the form
X(U)=M(U)U
where
M(U)
is a matrix of
p
-homogeneous
m
-operators in
\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}\right)^{2\times 2}
.
In particular, one has the Fourier expansion

X(U)=\begin{bmatrix}X(U)^{+}\\
X(U)^{-}\end{bmatrix},\qquad X(U)^{\sigma}\triangleq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\vec{\jmath},k,\vec{\sigma},-\sigma)\in{\mathfrak{T}}_{p+2}\end{subarray}}X_{\vec{\jmath},k}^{\vec{\sigma},\sigma}u_{\vec{\jmath}}^{\vec{\sigma}}e^{{\rm i}\sigma k},

where the Fourier restriction
\mathfrak{T}_{p+2}
is the set of momentum preserving indices, defined, for a given
q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{*}
, as

\mathfrak{T}_{q}\triangleq\left\{\left(\vec{\jmath},\vec{\sigma}\right)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{q}\times\{\pm\}^{q}\quad\textnormal{s.t.}\quad\vec{\sigma}\cdot\vec{\jmath}=0\right\}.

(3.19)

We denote

\Sigma_{p+1}^{N+1}\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}^{m}_{q}
the class of pluri-homogeneous vector fields.
The vector fields in
\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{p+1}^{-\varrho}
,
\varrho\geqslant 0
, are called smoothing.

Symbolic calculus.

Let

{\sigma}(D_{x},D_{\xi},D_{y},D_{\eta})\triangleq D_{\xi}D_{y}-D_{x}D_{\eta}

where
D_{x}\triangleq\frac{1}{{\rm i}}\partial_{x}
and
D_{\xi},D_{y},D_{\eta}
are similarly defined.
The following is Definition 3.11 in
[
20
]
.

Definition 3.14

(Asymptotic expansion of composition symbol)
.

Let
p
,
p^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{N}}
,
K,K^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
K^{\prime}\leqslant K
,
\varrho\geqslant 0
,
m,m^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
.
Consider symbols
a\in\Sigma\Gamma_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}[\epsilon_{0},N]
and
b\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{0},N]
. For
U
in
B_{{\sigma}}^{K}(I;\epsilon_{0})

we define, for
\varrho<{\sigma}-s_{0}
, the symbol

(a\#_{\varrho}b)\left(U;t,x,\xi\right)\triangleq\sum_{k=0}^{\varrho}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{{\rm i}}{2}{\sigma}\left(D_{x},D_{\xi},D_{y},D_{\eta}\right)\right)^{k}\Big[a(U;t,x,\xi)b(U;t,y,\eta)\Big]_{|_{\begin{subarray}{c}x=y,\xi=\eta\end{subarray}}}

modulo symbols in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m+m^{\prime}-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{0},N]
.

The symbol
a\#_{\varrho}b
belongs to
\Sigma\Gamma^{m+m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{0},N]
.
Moreover

a\#_{\varrho}b=ab+\frac{1}{2{\rm i}}\{a,b\}

up to a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m+m^{\prime}-2}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{0},N]
,
where

\{a,b\}\triangleq\partial_{\xi}a\ \partial_{x}b-\partial_{x}a\ \partial_{\xi}b

denotes the Poisson bracket.

The following result is proved in Proposition
3.12
in
[
20
]
.

Proposition 3.15

(Composition of Bony-Weyl operators)
.

Let
p,q,N,K,K^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
K^{\prime}\leqslant K
,
\varrho\geqslant 0
,
m,m^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
.
Consider symbols

a\in\Sigma{\Gamma}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]
and
b\in\Sigma{\Gamma}^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},q}[\epsilon_{0},N]
.
Then

{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U;t,x,\xi)\right)}\circ{\OpBWPlain\left(b(U;t,x,\xi)\right)}-{\OpBWPlain\left((a\#_{\varrho}b)(U;t,x,\xi)\right)}

is a smoothing operator in
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho+m+m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p+q}[\epsilon_{0},N]
.

We have the following result, see e.g. Lemma 7.2 in
[
20
]
.

Lemma 3.16

(Bony paraproduct decomposition)
.

Let
u_{1},u_{2}
be functions in
H^{\sigma}({\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{C}})
with

{\sigma}>\frac{1}{2}
. Then

u_{1}u_{2}={\OpBWPlain\left(u_{1}\right)}u_{2}+{\OpBWPlain\left(u_{2}\right)}u_{1}+R_{1}(u_{1})u_{2}+R_{2}(u_{2})u_{1}

where for
\mathsf{j}=1,2
,
R_{\mathsf{j}}
is a homogeneous smoothing operator in
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{1}
for any
\varrho\geqslant 0
.

We now state other composition results for
m
-operators which follow as in

[
27
, Proposition 2.15]
.

Proposition 3.17

(Compositions of
m
-operators)
.

Let
p,p^{\prime},N,K,K^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
K^{\prime}\leqslant K
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
. Let
m,m^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Then

1.

If
M(U;t)
is in

\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]
and
M^{\prime}(U;t)
is in

\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m^{\prime}}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{0},N]
then the composition

M(u;t)\circ M^{\prime}(U;t)

is in
\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m+\max(m^{\prime},0)}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{0},N]
.

2.

If
M(U)
is a homogeneous
m
-operator in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{p}^{m}

and
M^{(\ell)}(U;t)
,
\ell=1,\dots,p+1
, are matrices of
m_{\ell}
-operators in

\Sigma\mathcal{M}^{m_{\ell}}_{K,K^{\prime},q_{\ell}}[\epsilon_{0},N]
with
m_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

q_{\ell}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
then

M\left(M^{(1)}(U;t)u,\ldots,M^{(p)}(U;t)U\right)M^{(p+1)}(U;t)

belongs to
\Sigma\mathcal{M}_{K,K^{\prime},p+\bar{q}}^{m+\bar{m}}[\epsilon_{0},N]
with
\bar{m}\triangleq\sum_{\ell=1}^{p+1}\max(m_{\ell},0)
and
\bar{q}\triangleq\sum_{\ell=1}^{p+1}q_{\ell}
.

3.

Let
a
be a symbol in
{{\Sigma\Gamma}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0},N]}
with
m\geqslant 0
and
R
a smoothing operator in
{{\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{0},N]}
. Then

\displaystyle{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U;t,\cdot)\right)}\circ R(U;t)\,,\ \ R(U;t)\circ{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U;t,\cdot)\right)}\,\ \in{{\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho+m}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[\epsilon_{0},N]}.

4.

If
a_{p}
is in
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{p}^{m}
and
M(U)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{M}}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}[r,N]
then
a_{p}(M(U),U,\ldots,U;x,\xi)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}[r,N]
and

{\OpBWPlain\left(a(W,U,\ldots,U;x,\xi)\right)}_{|W=M(U)}={\OpBWPlain\left(a_{p}(M(U),U,\ldots,U;x,\xi)\right)}+R(U)

where
R(U)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}_{K,K^{\prime},p+p^{\prime}}^{-\varrho}[r,N]
, for any
\varrho>0
. In particular if
a\in\Sigma\Gamma_{K,K^{\prime},p}^{m}[r,N]
then

\displaystyle\text{ If}\quad\partial_{t}U=M_{0}(U)U,\quad M_{0}(U)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{M}}_{K,K^{\prime},0}^{m^{\prime}}[r,N],

\displaystyle\text{then}\quad\partial_{t}{\OpBWPlain\left(a(U;x,\xi)\right)}={\OpBWPlain\left(a_{[1]}(U;x,\xi)\right)}+R(U),

where
a_{[1]}\in\Sigma\Gamma_{K,K^{\prime}+1,p}^{m}[r,N]
and
R(U)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}_{K,K^{\prime},p^{\prime}}^{-\varrho}[r,N]

Notation 3.18
.

In the sequel if
K^{\prime}=0
we denote a
symbol
a(U;t,x,\xi)
in
\Gamma_{K,0,p}^{m}[\epsilon_{0}]
simply as

a(U;x,\xi)
,
and a smoothing operator in
R(U;t)

in

\Sigma\mathcal{R}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,p}[\epsilon_{0},N]
simply as
R(U)
,
without writing the
t
-dependence.

We finally provide the Bony paralinearization formula
of the composition operator whose proof is a combination of
[
20
, Lemma 3.19]
.

Lemma 3.19

(Bony Paralinearization formula)
.

Let
F
be a smooth
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued function defined on a neighborhood of zero in
{\mathbb{C}}
, vanishing at zero at order
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then there are
s_{0},\epsilon_{0}>0

such that if
u\in B_{H^{s_{0}}({\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{R}})}(\varepsilon_{0}),
then

\displaystyle F(u)=\ {\OpBWPlain\left(F^{\prime}\left(u\right)\right)}u+R\left(u\right)u,

where
R\left(u\right)
is a smoothing operator
in
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,q^{\prime}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
q^{\prime}\triangleq\max(q-1,1)
, for any
\varrho\geqslant 0
.

3.2
Spectrally localized maps

In this section we introduce the class of spectrally localized map, needed to apply the Darboux symplectic correction (see
Proposition

7.1
). This class was introduced first in
[
27
, Def. 2.15]

Definition 3.20

(Spectrally localized maps)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{R},p,N\in\mathbb{N},K,K^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
with
K^{\prime}\leqslant K
and
r>0
.

i)

Spectrally localized
p
-homogeneous maps.
We denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}
the subspace of
m
-operators
S(U)
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{m}_{p}
whose coefficients
S_{\vec{\jmath},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}}
(see (
3.14
)) satisfying the following spectral condition: there are
\delta>0,C>1
such that

S_{\vec{\jmath},j,k}^{\vec{\sigma}}\not=0\quad\implies|\vec{\jmath}|\leqslant\delta|j|,\quad C^{-1}|k|\leqslant|j|\leqslant C|k|.

We denote
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}\triangleq\bigcup_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{p}
and by
\Sigma^{N}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}
the class of pluri-homogeneous spectrally localized maps of the form
\sum_{q=p}^{N}S_{q}
with
S_{q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}
and
\Sigma_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}\triangleq\bigcup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\Sigma^{N}_{p}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q}
. For
p\geqslant N+1
we mean that the sum is empty.

ii)

Non-homogeneous spectrally localized maps.
We denote
\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0}]
the space of maps
(U,t,V)\mapsto S(U;t)V
defined on
B^{K^{\prime}}_{K}(I;r)\times I\times C^{0}(I,H^{s_{0}}(T,\mathbb{C}))
for some
s_{0}>0
, which are linear in the variable
V
and such that the following holds true. For any
s\in\mathbb{R}
there are
C>0
and
r(s)\in[0,\epsilon_{0}]
such that for any
U\in B^{K^{\prime}}_{K}(I;r(s))\cap C^{\ast}(I,H^{s}(T;\mathbb{C}^{2}))
, any
V\in C^{\ast}_{K-K^{\prime}}(I,H^{s}(T,\mathbb{C})),
any
0\leqslant k\leqslant K-K^{\prime},t\in I
, we have that

\displaystyle\|\partial^{k}_{t}(S(U;t)V)(t,\cdot)\|_{H^{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m}}\leqslant

\displaystyle C\sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\|U\|^{p}_{k^{\prime},s_{0}}\|V\|_{H^{k^{\prime\prime},s}},\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\ p\geqslant 1,

\displaystyle\|\partial^{k}_{t}(S(U;t)V)\|_{H^{s-\frac{3}{2}k-m}}\leqslant

\displaystyle C\|V\|_{k,s},\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\ p=0.

We denote
S^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},N}[\epsilon_{0}]\triangleq\bigcup_{p}\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[\epsilon_{0}]
.

iii)

Spectrally localized Maps.
We denote by
\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
, the space of maps
(U,t,V)\mapsto S(U;t)V
of the form

S(U;t)V=\sum_{q=p}^{N}S_{q}(U)V+S_{>N}(U;t)V,

where
S_{q}
are spectrally localized homogeneous maps in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{m}_{q},q=p,\ldots,N
and
S_{>N}
is a non-homogeneous spectrally localized map in
\mathcal{S}^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[\epsilon_{0}]
. We denote by
\left(\Sigma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]\right)^{2\times 2}
the space of
2\times 2
matrices whose entries are spectrally localized maps in
\Sigma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]
. We will use also the notation
\Sigma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N]\triangleq\bigcup_{l\geqslant 0}\Sigma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},p}[r,N+l]
.

3.3
z
-dependent paradifferential calculus

The following “Kernel-functions",
that depend on the "convolutive
2\pi
-periodic variable"
z
,
have to be considered as Taylor remainders of functions
K\left(u;x,z\right)
at
z=0

which are smooth in
u
and
which have finite regularity in
x
and
z
.
A Kernel function
is a
z
-dependent family of functions
(cfr. Definition
3.3
) with coefficients of size proportional to

|z|^{n}_{\mathbb{T}}
. For
n>-1
such singularity is integrable in
z
.

Definition 3.21

(Kernel functions)
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
p,N\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

K\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
\epsilon_{0}>0
.

i)

p
-
homogeneous Kernel-functions.
If
p\in{\mathbb{N}}

we denote
\widetilde{K{\mathcal{F}}}^{n}_{p}
the space of

z
-dependent,
p
-homogeneous maps
from
\dot{H}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}\right)
to the space of

x
-translation invariant real functions

\kappa(u;x,z)
of class
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}

in
(x,z)\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
with Fourier expansion

\kappa(u;x,z)=\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{p}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}}\!\kappa_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{p}}(z)u_{j_{1}}\cdots u_{j_{p}}e^{{\rm i}(j_{1}+\cdots+j_{p})x}\,,\quad z\in{\mathbb{T}}\setminus\{0\}\,,

with coefficients
\kappa_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{p}}(z)
of class
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T};{\mathbb{C}}\right)
,
symmetric in
(j_{1},\ldots,j_{p})
,
satisfying the reality condition

\overline{\kappa_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{p}}}\left(z\right)=\kappa_{-j_{1},\ldots,-j_{p}}\left(z\right)

and the
following: for any
l\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exist
\mu>0
and
a constant
C>0
such that

\left|\partial_{z}^{l}\kappa_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{p}}(z)\right|\leqslant C\left|\vec{\jmath}_{p}\right|^{\mu}\ \left|z\right|^{n-l}_{\mathbb{T}}\,,\quad\forall\vec{\jmath}_{p}=\left(j_{1},\ldots,j_{p}\right)\in({\mathbb{Z}}^{*})^{p}\,.

(3.20)

For
p=0
we denote by
\widetilde{K{\mathcal{F}}}^{n}_{0}
the space of
maps
z\mapsto\kappa(z)
which satisfy

\left|\partial_{z}^{l}\kappa(z)\right|\leqslant C\,\left|z\right|^{n-l}_{\mathbb{T}}
.

ii)

Non-homogeneous Kernel-functions.
We denote by
K{\mathcal{F}}_{K,0,p}^{n}[\epsilon_{0}]
the space of
z
-dependent,
real functions
\kappa(u;x,z)
,
defined for
u\in B_{s_{0}}^{0}(I;\epsilon_{0})
for some
s_{0}
large enough, such that for any
0\leqslant k\leqslant K
and
l\leqslant\max\left\{0,\left\lceil 1+n\right\rceil\big.\right\}
, any
s\geqslant s_{0}
, there are
C>0
,
0<\epsilon_{0}(s)<\epsilon_{0}
and for any
u\in B_{s_{0}}^{K}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}(s)\right)\cap C_{*}^{k}\left(I,\dot{H}^{s}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}\right)\right)
and any
{\mathtt{b}}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, with
\alpha\leqslant s-s_{0},
one has the estimate

\left|\partial_{t}^{k}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{z}^{l}\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)\right|\leqslant C\|u\|_{k,s_{0}}^{p-1}\|u\|_{k,s}\ \left|z\right|^{n-l}_{\mathbb{T}}\,,\qquad z\in\mathbb{T}\setminus\{0\}\,.

(3.21)

If
p=0
the right hand side in (
3.21
)
has to be replaced by
\left|z\right|^{n-l}_{\mathbb{T}}
.

iii)

Kernel-functions.
We denote by
\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}_{K,0,p}^{n}[\epsilon_{0},N]
the space of real functions
of the form

\kappa(u;x,z)=\sum_{q=p}^{N}\kappa_{q}\left(u;x,z\right)+\kappa_{>N}(u;x,z),

where
\kappa_{q}\left(u;x,z\right)
,
q=p,\dots,N

are homogeneous Kernel functions in
\widetilde{K{\mathcal{F}}}_{q}^{n}
, and

\kappa_{>N}(u;x,z)

is a non-homogeneous Kernel function in
K{\mathcal{F}}_{K,0,N+1}^{n}[\epsilon_{0}]
.

A Kernel function
\kappa(u;x,z)
is
real
if it is real valued for any

u\in B_{s_{0},{\mathbb{R}}}^{0}(I;\epsilon_{0})
.

We list some properties of the Kernel functions.
In view of the second point of Remark
3.2
,
a homogeneous Kernel function
\kappa(u;x,z)
in
\widetilde{K{\mathcal{F}}}^{n}_{p}

defines a non-homogeneous Kernel function in
K{\mathcal{F}}_{K,0,p}^{n}[\epsilon_{0}]

for any
\epsilon_{0}>0
.

Remark 3.22
.

Let us make the following remarks.

•

Let
\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)
be
a Kernel function in
\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
with
n\geqslant 0
,
which admits a continuous extension in
z=0
. Then its trace

\kappa\left(u;x,0\right)
at
z=0

is a function in
\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

•

If
\kappa(u;x,z)
is a homogeneous Kernel function
\widetilde{K{\mathcal{F}}}^{n}_{p}
,
the two definitions of quantization in (
3.9
) differ
by a Kernel smoothing operator in
\widetilde{K{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho,n}_{p}
, for any
\varrho>0
,
according to
Definition
3.25
below.

•

(Sum and product of Kernel functions)
If
\kappa_{1}(u;x,z)
is a Kernel function in

\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n_{1}}_{K,0,p_{1}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

and
\kappa_{2}(u;x,z)
in

\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n_{2}}_{K,0,p_{2}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
then the sum

(\kappa_{1}+\kappa_{2})(u;x,z)
is a Kernel function in
\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{\min\left\{n_{1},n_{2}\right\}}_{K,0,\min\left\{p_{1},p_{2}\right\}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and the product

(\kappa_{1}\kappa_{2})(u;x,z)
is a Kernel function in

\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n_{1}+n_{2}}_{K,0,p_{1}+p_{2}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

•

(Integral of Kernel functions)
Let
\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)
be a Kernel function in
\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
with
n>-1
. Then

\mathop{\,\hbox to0.0pt{--\hss}\!\!\!\int}\nolimits\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)\textnormal{d}z
is a function in

\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
This follows directly integrating (
3.20
) and (
3.21
) in
z
.

The
m
-Kernel-operators defined below are a
z
-dependent family of
m
-operators
(cfr. Definition
3.8
) with coefficients of size proportional to

|z|^{n}_{\mathbb{T}}
.
For
n>-1
such singularity is integrable in
z
.
A family of
z
-dependent paraproduct operators associated to
Kernel functions defines a
0
-Kernel operator, see Remark

3.24
. The kind of operators will appear only in case
m<0
, as smoothing operators
in the composition of Bony-Weyl quantizations of
Kernel-functions (see Definition
3.25
).

Definition 3.23
.

Let
m,n\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
p,N\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

K\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
\epsilon_{0}>0
.

i)

p
-homogeneous
m
-Kernel-operator.

We denote by
\widetilde{K\mathcal{M}}^{m,n}_{p}

the space of

z
-dependent,
x
-translation invariant homogeneous
m
-operators
according to
Definition

3.8
,
Item

i
, in which the constant

C
is substituted with
C\left|z\right|^{n}_{\mathbb{T}}
, equivalently

M(u;z)v\left(x\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{p+2}\\
j_{1}+\ldots+j_{p}+j=k\end{subarray}}M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}\left(z\right)u_{j_{1}}\ldots u_{j_{p}}v_{j}e^{{\rm i}kx}\,,\qquad z\in\mathbb{T}\setminus\{0\}\,,

(3.22)

with coefficients satisfying

\big|M_{\vec{\jmath}_{p},j,k}\left(z\right)\big|\leqslant C\ {\rm max}_{2}\left\{\left|j_{1}\right|,\ldots,\left|j_{p}\right|,\left|j\right|\right\}^{\mu}\ \max\left\{\left|j_{1}\right|,\ldots,\left|j_{p}\right|,\left|j\right|\right\}^{m}\left|z\right|^{n}_{\mathbb{T}}\,.

(3.23)

If
p=0
the right hand side of (
3.22
) is replaced by

\sum_{j\in{\bf Z}}M_{j}\left(z\right)v_{j}e^{{\rm i}jx}
with
\big|M_{j}\left(z\right)\big|\leqslant C\left|j\right|^{m}\left|z\right|^{n}_{\mathbb{T}}
.

ii)

Non-homogeneous
m
-Kernel-operator.

We denote by
K\mathcal{M}^{m,n}_{K,0,p}[\epsilon_{0}]

the space of
z
-dependent,
non-homogeneous operators
M(u;z)v
defined for any

z\in\mathbb{T}\setminus\{0\}
, such that for any
0\leqslant k\leqslant K

\left\|{\partial_{t}^{k}\left(M(u;z)v\right)}\right\|_{s-\alpha k-m}\\
\leqslant C\left|z\right|^{n}_{\mathbb{T}}\ \sum_{k^{\prime}+k^{\prime\prime}=k}\left(\|{v}\|_{k^{\prime\prime},s}\|{u}\|_{k^{\prime},{s_{0}}}^{p}+\|{v}\|_{k^{\prime\prime},{s_{0}}}\|u\|_{k^{\prime},{s_{0}}}^{p-1}\|u\|_{k^{\prime},s}\right)\,.

(3.24)

iii)

m
-Kernel-Operator.

We denote by
\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{m,n}_{K,0,p}[\epsilon_{0},N]

the space of operators
of the form

M(u;z)v=\sum_{q=p}^{N}M_{q}(u)v+M_{>N}(u;z)v

(3.25)

where
M_{q}
are homogeneous
m
-Kernel
operators in
\widetilde{K\mathcal{M}}^{m,n}_{q}
,
q=p,\ldots,N
and
M_{>N}
is a non–homogeneous
m
-Kernel-operator in

\mathcal{M}^{m,n}_{K,0,N+1}[\epsilon_{0}]
.
We denote
by
\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m}_{q}
the space
pluri-homogeneous
m
-Kernel operators of the form (
3.25
) with
M_{>N}=0
.

Remark 3.24
.

Given a Kernel function
\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)
in
\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
then
{\OpBWPlain\left(\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)\right)}
is
0
-
Kernel operator in
\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{0,n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

Definition 3.25

(Kernel-smoothing operators)
.

Given

\varrho>0
we define the homogeneous and non-homogeneous Kernel-smoothing operators as

\displaystyle\widetilde{K}{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n}_{p}\triangleq\widetilde{K}\mathcal{M}^{-\varrho,n}_{p},

\displaystyle K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right]\triangleq K\mathcal{M}^{-\varrho,n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right],

\displaystyle\Sigma K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\triangleq\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{-\varrho,n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

In view of
[
27
, Lemma 2.8]
,
if
M\left(u,\ldots,u;z\right)
is a homogeneous

m
-Kernel operator in
\widetilde{K}\mathcal{M}^{m,n}_{p}
then
M\left(u,\ldots,u;z\right)

defines a non-homogeneous
m
-Kernel operator in
K\mathcal{M}^{m,n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right]
for any
\epsilon_{0}>0
and
K\in\mathbb{N}
.

The classes of paraproducts associated to
Kernel functions and
m
-Kernel-operators are closed w.r.t.
compositions as we list below, cf.
[
19
]
.

Proposition 3.26

(Composition of
z
-dependent operators)
.

Let
m,n,m^{\prime},n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, and integers

K,p,p^{\prime},N\in\mathbb{N}
with
p,p^{\prime}\leqslant N
.

1.

Let
\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and
\kappa^{\prime}\left(u;x,z\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n^{\prime}}_{K,0,p^{\prime}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

be Kernel functions. Then

{\OpBWPlain\left(\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)\right)}\circ{\OpBWPlain\left(\kappa^{\prime}\left(u;x,z\right)\right)}={\OpBWPlain\left(\kappa\ \kappa^{\prime}\left(u;x,z\right)\right)}+R\left(u;z\right),

where
R\left(u;z\right)
is a Kernel-smoothing operator in
\Sigma K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n+n^{\prime}}_{K,0,p+p^{\prime}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
for any
\varrho\geqslant 0
;

2.

Let
M\left(u;z\right)
be a
m
-Kernel operator in

\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{m,n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and

M^{\prime}\left(u;z\right)
be an
m^{\prime}
-operator belonging to

\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{m^{\prime},n^{\prime}}_{K,0,p^{\prime}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
. Then
M\left(u;z\right)\circ M^{\prime}\left(u;z\right)
belongs to

\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{m+\max\left(m^{\prime},0\right),n+n^{\prime}}_{K,0,p+p^{\prime}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

3.

Let
\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)
be a Kernel function in

\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and
R\left(u;z\right)

be a Kernel smoothing operator in
\Sigma K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n^{\prime}}_{K,0,p^{\prime}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
then
{\OpBWPlain\left(\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)\right)}\circ R\left(u;z\right)
and
R\left(u;z\right)\circ{\OpBWPlain\left(\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)\right)}
are a Kernel smoothing operator in
\Sigma K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n+n^{\prime}}_{K,0,p+p^{\prime}}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

4.

Let
M\left(u;z\right)
be an homogeneous
m
-Kernel operator
in
\widetilde{K}\mathcal{M}^{m,n}_{1}
, and
M^{\prime}\left(u;z\right)
in
\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{0,0}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
then
M\left(M^{\prime}\left(u;z\right)u;z\right)\in\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{m,n}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

Finally integrating (
3.23
) and (
3.24
) in
z
we deduce the following lemma.

Lemma 3.27

(Integrals of Kernel smoothing operators)
.

Let
R\left(u;z\right)
be a Kernel smoothing operator belonging to
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

with
n>-1
.
Then

\int_{\mathbb{T}}R\left(u;z\right)g\left(x-z\right)\textnormal{d}z=R_{1}\left(u\right)g,\qquad\int_{\mathbb{T}}R\left(u;z\right)\textnormal{d}z=R_{2}\left(u\right),

where
R_{1}\left(u\right)
,
R_{2}\left(u\right)
are smoothing operators
in
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

The following proposition will be crucial in Section
4
, and is proved in
[
19
]
.

Proposition 3.28
.

Let
n>-1
and
\kappa\left(u;x,z\right)
be a Kernel-function in

\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
Let us define the operator, for any
g\in\dot{H}^{s}(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R})
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\left({\mathcal{T}}_{\kappa}g\right)\left(x\right)\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\kappa\left(u;\bullet,z\right)\right)}g\left(x-z\right)\textnormal{d}z\,.

Then there exists

•

a symbol
a\left(u;x,\xi\right)

in
\Sigma\Gamma^{-\left(1+n\right)}_{K,0,p}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
satisfying (
3.12
);

•

a pluri-homogeneous smoothing operator
R\left(u\right)

in
\Sigma_{p}^{N}\widetilde{{\mathcal{R}}}^{-\varrho}_{q}

for any
\varrho>0
;

such that
{\mathcal{T}}_{\kappa}g={\OpBWPlain\left(a\left(u;x,\xi\right)\right)}g+R\left(u\right)g
.

4
Paralinearization of the Kelvin-Helmholtz system

Notation 4.1
.

In the present section we use the following notation

B^{K}_{s,{\mathbb{R}}}\left(I;r\right)\triangleq B_{C^{K}_{\ast}\left(I;\dot{H}^{s}\left({\mathbb{T}};{\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)}\left(0,r\right)\ .

We warn the reader that such notation is conflictive with the notation introduced at page
3
, but we think that in the restricted context of the paralinearization procedure outlined here there is no risk of confusion and it helps to streamline the mathematical statements that we present.

In the present section we paralinearize the system
Eq.

1.9
in the
(\eta,\psi)
-variables. The result we obtain is the following one.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma\geqslant 0
,
{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varrho\geqslant 0
, for any
K\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
s_{0}>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that if
\eta,\psi\in B^{K}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
is a solution of
Eq.

1.9
then
\left(\eta,\psi\right)
solves the paradifferential equation

\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{t}\\
\psi_{t}\end{bmatrix}={\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{Q}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)+{\bm{B}}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|-{\rm i}V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\textnormal{Id}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}\ \xi+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix},

(4.1)

where

•

The matrix of symbols
{\bm{Q}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}
satisfy (
3.12
) and is given by

{\bm{Q}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\triangleq\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-\frac{\left|\xi\right|}{2}\\
\gamma\left(1+\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)\left(\left|\xi\right|^{2}-1\right)-\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{2}+w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\right)\left|\xi\right|+\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{\left(1+2\eta\right)}&0\end{array}\right]\in\left(\Sigma\Gamma^{2}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2},

(4.2)

with

\displaystyle\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\left(\frac{1+2\eta}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}-1\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.3)

\displaystyle w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\left(W_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)-{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

\displaystyle W_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\left(\psi_{x}+{\mathtt{b}}\right)\frac{1+2\eta}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

In particular,
W_{\mathtt{b}}\left(0,0;x\right)\equiv{\mathtt{b}}
;

•

{\bm{B}}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)&0\\
B_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)&-B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\end{array}\right]\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2},

(4.4)

where

B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\triangleq\left(\psi_{x}+{\mathtt{b}}\right)\frac{\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)}{1+2\eta}\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],\qquad\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\triangleq\frac{2\eta_{x}\left(1+2\eta\right)}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

In particular
{\bm{B}}_{{\mathtt{b}}}
satisfy (
3.12
);

•

V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and is explicitly defined as

V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right]-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\ ,

(4.5)

where
\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)
is defined in (
1.5
);

•

{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\in\left(\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
satisfies (
3.12
) and is explicitly defined as

{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\triangleq\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}A_{\left[0\right]}^{\eta}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)&A_{\left[-1\right]}^{\eta}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\\
A_{\left[0\right]}^{\psi}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)&A_{\left[-1\right]}^{\psi}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\end{array}\right],

with
A_{\left[m\right]}^{\mathsf{u}}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
for
\mathsf{u}=\eta,\psi
and
m\in\mathbb{R}
;

•

{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
and is real-valued.

Remark 4.3
.

Notice that the quasilinear contribution
-\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{2}+w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\right)\left|\xi\right|=\frac{W_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)}{2}\ \left|\xi\right|
is not nil when
{\mathtt{b}}=0
, as it is evident from
Eqs.

4.68
and
4.69
. This term, in particular, is an unstable contribution that is not present in the one-phase version of the present system, cf.
[
20
,
27
,
1
]
.

Notation 4.4
.

Along this section, for
\eta\in B^{K}_{s_{0},{\mathbb{R}}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
and
W\triangleq\begin{bmatrix}w_{1}\\
w_{2}\end{bmatrix}\in\left(B^{K}_{s_{0},{\mathbb{R}}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)\right)^{2}
, we use the notation

1.

For any
x,z\in\mathbb{T}
(cf. (
1.3
))

\displaystyle r=r\left(x\right)=r\left(\eta;x\right)=\sqrt{1+2\eta\left(x\right)}\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

\displaystyle\delta_{z}\eta\triangleq\eta\left(x\right)-\eta\left(x-z\right)\in\widetilde{K{\mathcal{F}}}_{1}^{\ 1}\ ;

(4.6)

2.

V\left(W;x\right)
is a generic element in
\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
(cf.
Definition

3.3
) and
V^{n}\left(W;x,z\right)
is a generic element in
\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{n}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
n>-1
(cf.
Definition

3.21
);

3.

A_{\left[m\right]}\left(W;x,\xi\right)
is a generic element in
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
for
m\in\mathbb{R}
that satisfies (
3.12
) and
{\bm{A}}_{\left[m\right]}\left(W;x,\xi\right)
is a generic element in
\left(\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
for
m\in\mathbb{R}
whose entries satisfy (
3.12
);

4.

R\left(W\right)
is a generic element in
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
which is real-valued and
R\left(W;z\right)
is a generic element in
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
n>-1
which is real-valued. Similarly
{\bm{R}}\left(W\right)
is a generic element in
\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
which is real-valued and
{\bm{R}}\left(W;z\right)
is a generic element in
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,n}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
n>-1
which is real-valued.

Remark 4.5
.

Accordingly to the notation introduced in
Notation

4.4
we write

{\bm{R}}\left(W\right)W\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}=R_{1}\left(W\right)w_{1}+R_{2}\left(W\right)w_{2},

where
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
are elements of the space
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
. The same holds when we write
{\bm{R}}\left(W;z\right)W\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}
.

Notice that from
Equation

1.5
we derive the relation

\mathpzc{H}\left(\eta\right)\omega=\eta_{x}\ \mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\omega+\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\omega,

(4.7)

where

\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\omega\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{\sqrt{1+2\eta\left(x\right)}\sqrt{1+2\eta\left(y\right)}\sin\left(x-y\right)}{1+\eta\left(x\right)+\eta\left(y\right)-\sqrt{1+2\eta\left(x\right)}\sqrt{1+2\eta\left(y\right)}\cos\left(x-y\right)}\ \omega\left(y\right)\textnormal{d}y.

(4.8)

4.1
Paralinearization of
\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)

The present section is dedicated to paralinearize the nonlocal operator
\mathpzc{H}_{0}(\eta)
given in (
4.8
).

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varrho\geqslant 0
, for any
K\in\mathbb{N}
there exist
s_{0}>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that if

\eta,g\in B^{K}_{s_{0},{\mathbb{R}}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
,
the nonlinear operator
\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)
in (
4.8
) admits the following paralinearization

1.

\displaystyle\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(-{\rm i}\left(1+\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)\sgn\xi+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}g

(4.9)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g}{1+2\eta}\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix},

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq-\frac{2\eta_{x}^{2}}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq-\frac{4\eta_{x}\left(1+2\eta\right)^{3}}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}}\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

A_{\left[m\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
satisfies (
3.12
) and
{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g\right)\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
is real-valued.

2.

\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[1\right]={\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)}{1+2\eta}\ \left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta,

(4.11)

where

•

\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and is explicitly defined in (
4.10
);

•

A_{\left[m\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
satisfying (
3.12
);

•

R\left(\eta\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and is real-valued.

Proof.

Part 1

(Proof of
Item

1
)
.

With the notation introduced in (
4.6
) we can rewrite (
4.8
) as

\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\mathsf{G}_{z}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)g\left(x-z\right)\textnormal{d}z=\mathpzc{H}g+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\mathsf{G}_{z}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{G}_{z}\left(0\right)\right)g\left(x-z\right)\textnormal{d}z,

(4.12)

where

\mathsf{G}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\triangleq\frac{\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\sin z}{{1-\mathsf{X}-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z}}\cdot

Let us now define the desingularization of
\mathsf{G}_{z}

\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\triangleq\mathsf{G}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\ 2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)\ 2\tan\left(z/2\right)=\frac{\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\ \sin\left(z/2\right)}\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{2}}{{1-2\mathsf{X}\ \sin\left(z/2\right)-\sqrt{1-4\mathsf{X}\ \sin\left(z/2\right)}\cos z}},

(4.13)

so that

\left(\mathsf{G}_{z}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{G}_{z}\left(0\right)\right)g\left(x-z\right)=\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{2\tan\left(z/2\right)}\cdot

(4.14)

Notice that from (
4.13
) we derive

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)=

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathsf{X}\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{4}}{{\left(1-2\,\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)-\sqrt{1-4\,\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cos\left(z\right)\right)^{2}}\sqrt{1-4\,\mathsf{X}\sin\left(z/2\right)}}\cdot

(4.15)

We need the following technical result whose proof is postponed at page
4.1
:

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\mathsf{K}_{z}(\mathsf{X})
, be as in
Eq.

4.13
. Then

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)-2\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.16)

are Kernel functions, which admit the expansions

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)-2=

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)+\mathsf{K}^{1}\left(\eta;x\right)\ 2\tan\left(z/2\right)+V^{2}\left(\eta;x,z\right),

(4.17)

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)+\mathsf{K}^{\prime 1}\left(\eta;x\right)\ \sin z+V^{2}\left(\eta;x,z\right),

where
\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)
and
\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)
are functions in
{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
having the expressions (
4.10
).Then,

\mathsf{K}^{1}\left(\eta;x\right)
and
\mathsf{K}^{\prime 1}\left(\eta;x\right)
are functions in
\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
while
V^{2}\left(\eta;x,z\right)
are Kernel-functions in
\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{2}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
as per
Notation

4.4
.

Bony paraproduct decomposition (cf.
Lemma

3.16
) give us that

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)g\left(x-z\right)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)}g\left(x-z\right)+{\OpBWPlain\left(g\left(x-z\right)\right)}\left[\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right]

(4.18)

\displaystyle+R_{1}\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)g\left(x-z\right)+R_{2}\left(g\left(x-z\right)\right)\left[\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right].

In view of (
4.16
) we can apply
Proposition

3.26
,
Items

4
and
2
and obtain that

\displaystyle R_{1}\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)g\left(x-z\right)=R\left(\eta;z\right)g,

\displaystyle R_{2}\left(g\left(x-z\right)\right)\left[\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right]=R\left(\eta;z\right)g,

(4.19)

for suitable
R\left(\bullet;z\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.
We use now Bony paralinearization formula of Lemma
3.19
and Bony paraproduct decomposition and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2

(4.20)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[{\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-2}\right)}\Delta_{z}\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(\Delta_{z}\eta\right)}\left[r^{-2}-1\right]+R_{1}\left(r^{-2}-1\right)\Delta_{z}\eta+R_{2}\left(\Delta_{z}\eta\right)\left[r^{-2}-1\right]\right]

\displaystyle+R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\cdot

Then, we apply again
Lemma

3.19
,
Proposition

3.26
,
Items

4
and
2
in order to get that

\displaystyle{\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left(R_{1}\left(r^{-2}-1\right)\Delta_{z}\eta+R_{2}\left(\Delta_{z}\eta\right)\left[r^{-2}-1\right]\right)=R\left(\eta;z\right)\eta,

(4.21)

\displaystyle R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}=R\left(\eta;z\right)\eta.

(4.22)

Besides, combining
Proposition

3.26
,
Item

1
and
Lemma

3.19
we obtain that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left({\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-2}\right)}\Delta_{z}\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(\Delta_{z}\eta\right)}\left[r^{-2}-1\right]\right)

(4.23)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\Delta_{z}\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(\tilde{V}^{0}\left(\eta;x,z\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta,g;z\right)\eta,

where

\tilde{V}^{0}(\eta;x,z)\triangleq r^{-2}\big(K_{z}^{\prime}(X)X\big)|_{X=\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}}.

We plug
Eqs.

4.23
and
4.21
in
Eq.

4.20
and the resulting equation and
Eq.

4.19
in
Eq.

4.18
and obtain, after using

\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{\tan(z/2)}\textnormal{d}z=0

and applying
Proposition

3.26
,
Item

1
, that

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)g\left(x-z\right)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)}g\left(x-z\right)+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{r^{2}}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\Delta_{z}\eta

(4.24)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(V^{0}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g;z\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix},

where

V^{0}(\eta,g;x,z)\triangleq\tilde{V}^{0}(\eta;x,z)g(x-z).

We Taylor expand in
z

V^{0}(\eta,g;x,z)=\overline{V}_{0}(\eta,g;x)+\overline{V}_{1}(\eta,g;x,z),\qquad\overline{V}_{1}\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

We thus plug (
4.24
) in (
4.14
) and insert the resulting equation in (
4.12
) and, after application of
3.22
and
3.27
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g

\displaystyle=\mathpzc{H}g+\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)}\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{2\tan\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z

(4.25)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{r^{2}}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin\left(z/2\right)\ \tan\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(V\left(\eta,g;x\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g;z\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix},

where

V(\eta,g;x)\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{\overline{V}_{1}(\eta,g;x,z)}{2\tan(z/2)}\textnormal{d}z.

We use now the identity
\frac{1}{4\sin\left(z/2\right)\ \tan\left(z/2\right)}=\frac{1}{4\sin^{2}\left(z/2\right)}-\frac{1}{8\cos^{2}\left(z/4\right)}
and
3.28
and
3.22
in order to transform

\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{r^{2}}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin\left(z/2\right)\ \tan\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z\\
=\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{r^{2}}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin^{2}\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z+{\OpBWPlain\left(V\left(\eta,g;x\right)+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta,g\right)\eta,

(4.26)

so that plugging (
4.26
) in (

) we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g

\displaystyle=\mathpzc{H}g+\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)}\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{2\tan\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z

(4.27)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{r^{2}}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin^{2}\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(V\left(\eta,g;x\right)+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g;z\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

Now, we can use the Taylor-like expansions (
4.17
),
Proposition

3.28
and the fact that
g
has zero average and obtain that

\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)}\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{2\tan\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z={\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\mathpzc{H}g+{\OpBWPlain\left(A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}g+R\left(\eta,g\right)g.

(4.28)

Next, denoting

\tilde{\mathsf{K}}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right)\triangleq\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{1+2\eta}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)-\frac{g}{1+2\eta}\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

we Taylor-expand

obtaining

\tilde{\mathsf{K}}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right)=\tilde{\mathsf{K}}^{1}\left(\eta,g;x\right)\sin z+V^{2}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right),

so that applying
3.22
and
3.28
we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{r^{2}}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin^{2}\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z

(4.29)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g}{1+2\eta}\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\left|D\right|\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(\tilde{\mathsf{K}}^{1}\left(\eta,g;x\right)\right)}\mathpzc{H}\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(V\left(\eta,g;x\right)+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta,g\right)\eta.

We plug
Eqs.

and

in

and use
