3.15
and obtain the desired quasi-linear expansion

\displaystyle\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(1+\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\mathpzc{H}g+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g}{1+2\eta}\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\left|D\right|\eta

(4.30)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}g+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g;z\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix},

thus (
4.9
) can be derive applying
Proposition

3.15
to (
4.30
) combined with
\mathpzc{H}={\OpBWPlain\left(-{\rm i}\sgn{\xi}\right)}
.

Part 2

(Proof of
Item

2
)
.

From
Eqs.

4.12
and
4.14
we have that

\mathpzc{H}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[1\right]=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2\right)\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{2\tan\left(z/2\right)}\cdot

(4.31)

Thus, we apply
Lemma

3.19
and obtain that

\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-2={\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right]+R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right].

(4.32)

Notice that from
Proposition

3.26
,
Item

4
we have that
R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and by Taylor expansion we have that

\tilde{R}\left(\eta;x,z\right)\triangleq R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-R\left(\frac{\eta_{x}}{r^{2}}\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho,1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

and, since
\frac{\Delta_{z}}{r^{2}}\in\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{1,0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
applying
Proposition

3.26
,
Item

2
we obtain that

\tilde{R}\left(\eta;x,z\right)\circ\frac{\Delta_{z}}{r^{2}}\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho+1,1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.33)

so that, thanks to (
4.33
), we have that

\int_{\mathbb{T}}R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right]\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{2\tan\left(z/2\right)}=R\left(\frac{\eta_{x}}{r^{2}}\right)\left[\frac{1}{r^{2}}\ \int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin\left(z/2\right)\tan\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z\right]+\int_{\mathbb{T}}R\left(\eta;x,z\right)\textnormal{d}z\ \eta.

Next we use the fact that

\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin\left(z/2\right)\tan\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z=m_{1}\left(D\right)\eta,\qquad\textnormal{with}\qquad m_{1}\left(\xi\right)\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{1}_{0},

the fact that
r^{-2}m_{1}\left(D\right)\in\Sigma K\mathcal{M}^{1,0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,
Proposition

3.26
,
Item

2
and
Lemma

3.27
to obtain that

\int_{\mathbb{T}}R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right]\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{2\tan\left(z/2\right)}=R\left(\eta\right)\eta.

(4.34)

Next, computations similar to the ones performed in
Eqs.

4.20
and
4.23
allow us to deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right]\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{2\tan\left(z/2\right)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin\left(z/2\right)\tan\left(z/2\right)}\right]\textnormal{d}z

(4.35)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(V\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta.

Taylor-expanding as in (
4.29
) and using
Proposition

3.15
we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin\left(z/2\right)\tan\left(z/2\right)}\right]\textnormal{d}z

(4.36)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\left|D\right|\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-2}\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta.

Combining
Equations

4.32
,
4.34
,
4.35
and
4.36

we obtain (
4.11
).

∎

Proof of
Lemma

4.7
.

Let us prove at first (
4.16
). We prove the result for
\mathsf{K}_{z}
only being the procedure for
\mathsf{K}_{z}^{\prime}
the same. Notice that from
Eq.

4.13
we immediately have that the map
\left(z,\mathsf{X}\right)\mapsto\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)
is analytic in
\mathbb{T}\times\left(-\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{4}\right)
.
Let us now define

\displaystyle\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{z}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)\triangleq\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\mathsf{y}}{1+2\mathsf{x}}\right),

which is again analytic for
\left|\mathsf{x}\right|,\left|\mathsf{y}\right|<\varepsilon_{1}
small. By analyticity we have that

\displaystyle\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{z}\left(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\right)=\sum_{p_{1},p_{2}=0}^{\infty}\underbrace{\frac{\partial_{\mathsf{x}}^{p_{2}}\partial_{\mathsf{y}}^{p_{1}}\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{z}\left(0,0\right)}{p_{1}!p_{2}!}}_{\triangleq\mathsf{k}_{p_{1},p_{2}}\left(z\right)}\mathsf{x}^{p_{2}}\mathsf{y}^{p_{1}},

with

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\mathsf{x}}^{p_{2}}\partial_{\mathsf{y}}^{p_{1}}\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{z}\left(0,0\right)\right|\leqslant Cp_{1}!p_{2}!\ \varepsilon_{1}^{-\left(p_{1}+p_{2}\right)}.

From
Eq.

4.13
it is immediate that

\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(0\right)=2,

so that

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)=\overline{\mathsf{K}}_{z}\left(\eta,\Delta_{z}\eta\right)=

\displaystyle\ 2+\sum_{p\geqslant 1}\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}\geqslant 1\\
p_{1}+p_{2}=p\end{subarray}}\mathsf{k}_{p_{1},p_{2}}\left(z\right)\ \eta^{p_{2}}\left(\Delta_{z}\eta\right)^{p_{1}}}_{{=:\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{p}\left(f;x,z\right)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 2+\sum_{p=1}^{N}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{p}\left(\eta;x,z\right)+\underbrace{\sum_{p>N}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{p}\left(\eta;x,z\right)}_{=:\mathsf{K}^{>N}\left(\eta;x,z\right)}\,.

We claim that for any
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell=0,\ldots,7
,

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{\ell}\widetilde{\mathsf{K}}^{p}\left(\eta;x,z\right)\

\displaystyle\in\widetilde{K}{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{p},

(4.37)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{\ell}\mathsf{K}^{>N}\left(\eta;x,z\right)

\displaystyle\in K{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{K,0,N+1}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right].

(4.38)

The proof of
Eqs.

4.37
and
4.38
is the same as the one performed in order to prove
[
19
, Eqs. (4.34) and (4.35)]
and is thus omitted. The proof for (
4.16
) for
\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)
is the same as the one performed for
\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)
with the sole difference that
\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(0\right)=0
.

The proof of (
4.17
) follows the lines of the proof of
[
19
, Eq. (4.23)]
, and is generic enough to be applied in the present case. It remains to prove
Eq.

4.10
, we can Taylor-expand in
z=0
and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{0}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)+\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\right|_{z=0}z+R_{1}\left(\mathsf{K};\mathsf{X}\right)\left(z\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{0}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)+\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\right|_{z=0}z+R_{1}\left(\mathsf{K}^{\prime};\mathsf{X}\right)\left(z\right).

Explicit computations show that

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{0}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle 2-\frac{2\mathsf{X}^{2}}{1+\mathsf{X}^{2}},

\displaystyle\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\right|_{z=0}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{4\mathsf{X}^{3}}{\left(1+\mathsf{X}^{2}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{0}^{\prime}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{4\mathsf{X}}{\left(1+\mathsf{X}^{2}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\right|_{z=0}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{4\mathsf{X}^{2}\left(3-\mathsf{X}^{2}\right)}{\left(1+\mathsf{X}^{2}\right)^{3}}\cdot

Hence setting
\mathsf{X}\triangleq\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}
and Taylor expanding we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}_{0}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)=

\displaystyle 2\underbrace{-2\frac{\eta_{x}^{2}}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}}_{\triangleq\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)}+4\frac{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}\eta_{x}\eta_{xx}}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}}z+V^{2}\left(\eta;x,z\right),

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{0}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)=

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\frac{4\left(1+2\eta\right)\eta_{x}}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}}}_{\triangleq\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)}+2\frac{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}-3\eta_{x}^{2}\right)\left(1+2\eta\right)^{3}\eta_{xx}}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{3}}z+V^{2}\left(\eta;x,z\right),

\displaystyle\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{x}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)\right|_{z=0}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{4\left(1+2\eta\right)\eta_{x}^{3}}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}}+R_{0}\left(\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{x}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)\right|_{z=0};x\right)\left(z\right),

\displaystyle\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{x}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)\right|_{z=0}\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{4\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}\eta_{x}^{2}\left(3\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}-\eta_{x}^{2}\right)}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{3}}+R_{0}\left(\left.\partial_{z}\mathsf{K}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{x}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)\right|_{z=0};x\right)\left(z\right),

so that defining

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{1}\left(\eta;x\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\ \frac{4\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}\eta_{x}\eta_{xx}}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}}-\frac{4\left(1+2\eta\right)\eta_{x}^{3}}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}}=\frac{4\left(1+2\eta\right)\eta_{x}\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)\eta_{xx}-\eta_{x}^{2}\right)}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime 1}\left(\eta;x\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\ \frac{2\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}-3\eta_{x}^{2}\right)\left(1+2\eta\right)^{3}\eta_{xx}-4\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}\eta_{x}^{2}\left(3\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}-\eta_{x}^{2}\right)}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{3}},

which are analytic applications w.r.t.
\eta
small in
W^{2,\infty}
, proving (
4.17
) and (
4.10
). ∎

4.2
Paralinearization of
\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)

Here we perform the paralinearization of the nonlinear operator
\mathpzc{D}_{0}(\eta)
introduced in (
1.5
).

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varrho\geqslant 0
, for any
K\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
s_{0}>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that if

\eta,g\in B^{K}_{s_{0},{\mathbb{R}}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)

and
\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)
be as in (
1.5
), we have that

1.

\displaystyle\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(-{\rm i}\ \frac{\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)}{r^{2}}\sgn{\xi}+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}g

(4.39)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g}{r^{4}}\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix},

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{2\eta_{x}\left(1+2\eta\right)}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.40)

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\frac{2\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}-\eta_{x}^{2}\right)}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}}\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.41)

A_{\left[m\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and

{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g\right)\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
.

2.

\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[1\right]=1+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{2}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}}+\frac{\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)}{r^{4}}\ \left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta\ ,

(4.42)

where

•

\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)
is explicitly defined in (
4.41
), and is such that
\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(0;x\right)\equiv 2
;

•

A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

R\left(\eta\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

Proof.

Part 1

(Proof of
Proposition

4.8
,
Item

1
)
.

Let us recall that
\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)
is defined in (
1.5
).
We proceed analogously as in the previous section. We have, using the auxiliary functions defined in (
4.6
) and the fact that
g
is of zero average, that

\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g=\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left(\mathsf{H}_{z}\left(\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)-\mathsf{H}_{z}\left(0\right)\right)\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z\,,

(4.43)

where

\mathsf{H}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\triangleq\frac{1-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z}{1-\mathsf{X}-\sqrt{1-2\mathsf{X}}\cos z}\ 2\sin\left(z/2\right).

We define

\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\right)\triangleq\mathsf{H}_{z}\left(\mathsf{X}\ 2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right).

(4.44)

We have the following technical result:

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\mathsf{J}_{z}(\mathsf{X})
, be as in
Eq.

4.44
. Then

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\triangleq\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)-2\sin\left(z/2\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)\in\Sigma K{\mathcal{F}}^{0}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.45)

are Kernel functions, which admit the expansion

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)-2\sin\left(z/2\right)=

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)+\mathsf{J}^{1}\left(\eta;x\right)\ 2\sin\left(z/2\right)+V^{2}\left(\eta;x,z\right),

(4.46)

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)+\mathsf{J}^{\prime 1}\left(\eta;x\right)\sin z+V^{2}\left(\eta;x,z\right),

where
\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right),\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)
are defined in (
4.40
)-(
4.41
) and
\mathsf{J}^{1}\left(\eta;x\right),\mathsf{J}^{\prime 1}\left(\eta;x\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

The proof of
Lemma

4.9
follows exactly the same lines of the proof of
Lemma

4.7
(cf. page
4.1
) and is thus omitted for the sake of brevity.

We apply
Lemma

3.16
and obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)_{\geqslant 1}g\left(x-z\right)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}_{\geqslant 1}g\left(x-z\right)+{\OpBWPlain\left(g\left(x-z\right)\right)}\left[\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\right]

(4.47)

\displaystyle+R_{1}\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\right)g\left(x-z\right)+R_{2}\left(g\left(x-z\right)\right)\left[\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\right].

In view of (
4.45
) we can apply
Proposition

3.26
,
Items

4
and
2
and obtain that

\displaystyle R_{1}\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\right)g\left(x-z\right)=R\left(\eta,g;z\right)g,

\displaystyle R_{2}\left(g\left(x-z\right)\right)\left[\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\right]=R\left(\eta,g;z\right)\eta.

(4.48)

Then, we apply
Lemmas

3.19
,
3.16
and
3.15
in a similar fashion to the procedure detailed in the previous section and obtain that

{\OpBWPlain\left(g\left(x-z\right)\right)}\left[\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\right]={\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{r^{4}}\mathsf{J}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\Delta_{z}\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(V^{0}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta,g;z\right)\eta.

(4.49)

Next, we use the expansions of
Lemma

4.9
and obtain that

\displaystyle{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{1+2\eta}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\right)}g\left(x-z\right)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)}{r^{2}}+V^{1}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right)\right)}g\left(x-z\right),

(4.50)

\displaystyle{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{r^{4}}\mathsf{J}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\Delta_{z}\eta=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g}{r^{4}}\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\Delta_{z}\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(V\left(\eta,g;x\right)+V^{1}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right)\right)}\delta_{z}\eta,

\displaystyle{\OpBWPlain\left(V^{0}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right)\right)}\eta=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\OpBWPlain\left(V\left(\eta,g;x\right)+V^{1}\left(\eta,g;x,z\right)\right)}\eta.

We plug the results in
Eqs.

4.50
,
4.49
,
4.48
,
4.47
and
4.44
into
Eq.

4.43
and obtain, after an application of
Proposition

3.28
, that

\displaystyle\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)}{r^{2}}\right)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{g\left(x-z\right)}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g}{r^{4}}\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{4\sin^{2}\left(z/2\right)}\textnormal{d}z

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}g+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g;z\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix},

from which we conclude after standard symbolic manipulations the identity in (
4.39
).

Part 2

(Proof of
Proposition

4.8
,
Item

2
)
.

Arguing similarly as it was done in order to deduce (
4.43
) and using
Eqs.

4.44
and
4.46
we have that

\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[1\right]=r^{-2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\mathsf{J}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)_{\geqslant 1}\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cdot

(4.51)

We apply
Lemma

3.19
and obtain that

\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[1\right]=r^{-2}+\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}\left\{{\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{J}^{\prime}_{z}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right]+R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right]\right\}\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}\cdot

(4.52)

Thus, computations similar to the ones that lead to (
4.34
) give us that

\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}R\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right]\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}=R\left(\eta\right)\eta.

(4.53)

Next, similar computations to the ones that lead to (
4.35
) give us that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{r^{2}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{J}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right]\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{2\sin\left(z/2\right)}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-4}\mathsf{J}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{2}}\right]\textnormal{d}z

(4.54)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(V\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta,

so that arguing as in order to deduce (
4.36
)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}}{\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-4}\mathsf{J}_{z}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Delta_{z}\eta}{r^{2}}\right)\right)}\left[\frac{\delta_{z}\eta}{\left(2\sin\left(z/2\right)\right)^{2}}\right]\textnormal{d}z

(4.55)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-4}\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)}\left|D\right|\eta+{\OpBWPlain\left(A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(r^{-4}\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta.

Thus, combining
Eqs.

4.51
,
4.52
,
4.53
,
4.54
and
4.55
and the paralinearization
r^{-2}=1+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{2}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}}\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta
derived using
Lemma

3.19
we obtain
Eq.

4.42
.

∎

4.3
Paralinearization of
\mathpzc{H}\left(\eta\right)

In view of (
4.7
), putting together the paralinearizations of
Sections

4.1
and
4.2
, we prove the following.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varrho\geqslant 0
, for any
K\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
s_{0}>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that if

\eta,g\in B^{K}_{s_{0},{\mathbb{R}}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
,
let
\mathpzc{H}\left(\eta\right)
be as in (
4.7
) then we have that

1.

\mathpzc{H}\left(\eta\right)g={\OpBWPlain\left(-{\rm i}\ \sgn{\xi}+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}g\\
+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{g}{1+2\eta}\ \mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\ \left|\xi\right|+2V_{0}\left(\eta,g;x\right){\rm i}\xi+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix},

(4.56)

where

•

\mathsf{J}^{0}
\left(\eta;x\right)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and is explicitly defined in (
4.40
);

•

V_{0}\left(\eta,\partial_{x}^{-1}g;x\right)\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g\ \in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, cf. (
1.5
);

•

A_{\left[m\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

R\left(\eta,g\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

2.

\mathpzc{H}\left(\eta\right)\left[1\right]={\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)}{1+2\eta}\ \left|\xi\right|+{\rm i}\ \tilde{V}\left(\eta;x\right)\xi+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\left[\eta\right],

(4.57)

where

•

\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and is explicitly defined in (
4.40
);

•

\tilde{V}\left(\eta;x\right)\triangleq\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[1\right]\ \in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
with
\tilde{V}\left(0;x\right)\equiv 1
;

•

A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

R\left(\eta\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

Proof.

We use now the expression in
Eq.

4.7
and
Propositions

4.8
and
4.6
as well as
Proposition

3.15
and obtain that

\mathpzc{H}\left(\eta\right)g={\OpBWPlain\left(-{\rm i}\ \left(1+\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)+\frac{\eta_{x}}{r^{2}}\ \mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)\sgn\xi+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}g\\
+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{g}{r^{2}}\left(\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)+\frac{\eta_{x}}{r^{2}}\ \mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)\left|\xi\right|+{\rm i}\ V_{0}\left(\eta,\partial_{x}^{-1}g;x\right)\xi+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,g;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,g\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
g\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

(4.58)

Notice that in order to deduce (
4.58
) we used the fact that
\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, which stems immediately from the paralinearization provided in
Proposition

4.8
,
Item

1
, next we relabeled
\frac{1}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)g=V_{0}\left(\eta,\partial_{x}^{-1}g;x\right)
.
Next we use
Equations

4.10
,
4.40
and
4.41
and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)+\frac{\eta_{x}}{r^{2}}\ \mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)+\frac{\eta_{x}}{r^{2}}\ \mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)=

\displaystyle-\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right).

Thus, transforming (
4.58
) into (
4.56
). The proof of (
4.57
) is almost identical to the proof of (
4.56
) and is hence omitted.
∎

4.4
Proof of
Theorem

4.2

We can finally paralinearize
Eq.

1.9
.
We use
Equations

4.56
and
4.57
, we set
g=\partial_{x}\psi
and
Proposition

3.15
in order to obtain, from (

), that

\displaystyle\eta_{t}

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{\left|\xi\right|}{2}+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\psi

(4.59)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{1}{2}B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|-{\rm i}\ V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\xi+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta,\psi\right)\left[\eta+\psi\right],

where

\displaystyle B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\triangleq B_{0}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)+{\mathtt{b}}\tilde{B}\left(\eta;x\right),

\displaystyle B_{0}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\triangleq\frac{\psi_{x}}{1+2\eta}\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{B}\left(\eta;x\right)\triangleq\frac{\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)}{1+2\eta}

(4.60)

and

V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right]-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}.

Let us now paralinearize the term

-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right].

We apply
Lemma

3.16
and obtain that

\displaystyle-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right)}\left[\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right]-{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]}{2}\right)}\left[\psi_{x}\right]

(4.61)

\displaystyle+R_{1}\left(-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right)\left[\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right]+R_{2}\left(\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right)\left[-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right].

Recall that
\frac{\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]}{2}=V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)
, so that we can apply
Propositions

3.17
and
3.15
and obtain that

\begin{gathered}{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]}{2}\right)}\left[\psi_{x}\right]=\ {\OpBWPlain\left(-{\rm i}\ V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\xi\right)}\psi+R\left(\eta,\psi\right)\psi,\\
R_{1}\left(-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right)\left[\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right]+R_{2}\left(\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right)\left[-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right]=\ {\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.\end{gathered}

(4.62)

Next we apply the paralinearization stated in
Proposition

4.8
and the composition theorems in
Propositions

3.17
and
3.15
and obtain that

{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right)}\left[\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right]={\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\psi_{x}}{1+2\eta}\ \mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\psi\\
+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\psi_{x}\left({\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right)}{1+2\eta}\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix},

(4.63)

so, using (
4.60
), we obtain that

{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right)}\left[\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right]={\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{1}{2}B_{0}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\psi\\
+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\psi_{x}\left({\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right)}{1+2\eta}\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

(4.64)

We invoke now
Proposition

4.8
and obtain, using the notation introduced in (
4.60
), that

-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right]={\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\tilde{B}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\psi\\
-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{2}+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{\left(1+2\eta\right)}-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\frac{{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}}\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

(4.65)

Notice that using
Eqs.

4.62
and
4.61
we obtain that

\displaystyle-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right]+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right)}\left[\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right]

(4.66)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(-{\rm i}\ V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\xi\right)}\psi+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

From
Equations

4.40
and
4.41
we obtain the relation

\mathsf{J}^{\prime 0}\left(\eta;x\right){=\frac{2\left(1+2\eta\right)^{4}}{\left(\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}\right)^{2}}-\left(\mathsf{J}^{0}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)^{2}}

(4.67)

so that using
Eqs.

4.60
,
4.67
,
4.64
and
4.65
and denoting with

\displaystyle W_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\triangleq W_{0}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)+{\mathtt{b}}\tilde{W}\left(\eta;x\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.68)

where

\displaystyle W_{0}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\triangleq\frac{\psi_{x}\left(1+2\eta\right)}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

\displaystyle\tilde{W}\left(\eta;x\right)\triangleq\frac{1+2\eta}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(4.69)

we obtain that

-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right]+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{\psi_{x}}{2}\right)}\left[\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\partial_{x}\psi\right]\right]=-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{2}+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{1}{2}B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\psi\\
+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{\left(1+2\eta\right)}-\frac{1}{2}\left(W_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)-B_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

(4.70)

We thus insert (
4.70
) in (
4.66
) and obtain that

\displaystyle-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}}{2}\mathpzc{D}_{0}\left(\eta\right)\left[{\mathtt{b}}+\psi_{x}\right]=

\displaystyle-\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{2}+{\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{1}{2}B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|-{\rm i}\ V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\xi+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\psi

(4.71)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{\left(1+2\eta\right)}-\frac{1}{2}\left(W_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)-B_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta

\displaystyle+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

An iterated application of
Lemma

3.19
give us that

\displaystyle-\gamma\mathpzc{K}\left(\eta\right)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(\gamma\left(1+\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)\left(\left|\xi\right|^{2}-1\right)\right)}\eta+R\left(\eta\right)\eta,

(4.72)

\displaystyle\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)

\displaystyle\triangleq\left(\frac{1+2\eta}{\left(1+2\eta\right)^{2}+\eta_{x}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}-1\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

We can thus now plug
Eqs.

4.72
and
4.71
in the second equation of (
1.9
) (recall that
\Omega
is set in (
2.22
) in order to cancel the 0-homogeneous components of the transport) and obtain that

\displaystyle\psi_{t}

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(-\frac{1}{2}B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|-{\rm i}\ V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\xi+A_{\left[-2\right]}\left(\eta;x,\xi\right)\right)}\psi

(4.73)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(\gamma\left(1+\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)\right)\left(\left|\xi\right|^{2}-1\right)+\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{\left(1+2\eta\right)}-\frac{1}{2}\left(W_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)-B_{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\right)\left|\xi\right|+A_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\eta

\displaystyle+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\cdot\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\end{bmatrix}.

Finally, we combine
Eqs.

4.73
and
4.59
to obtain (
4.1
) after a renaming, if needed.

5
Complex Hamiltonian formulation of the Kelvin-Helmholtz equations

We begin with the real Hamiltonian system (
2.5
), and introduce the following associated complex variables

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}\triangleq\mathpzc{C}\begin{bmatrix}v\\
\bar{v}\end{bmatrix}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathpzc{C}\triangleq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-{\rm i}&{\rm i}\end{bmatrix}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathpzc{C}^{-1}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{bmatrix}1&{\rm i}\\
1&-{\rm i}\end{bmatrix}.

(5.1)

Under this change of variables (
2.5
) is equivalent to

\begin{bmatrix}v_{t}\\
\bar{v}_{t}\end{bmatrix}=\mathpzc{C}^{-1}{\bm{J}}\nabla_{\left(\eta,\psi\right)}H\left(\mathpzc{C}\begin{bmatrix}v\\
\bar{v}\end{bmatrix}\right).

(5.2)

Defining
H_{\mathbb{C}}\triangleq H\circ\mathpzc{C}
and noting that

\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{\eta}\\
\partial_{\psi}\end{bmatrix}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
{\rm i}&-{\rm i}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\partial_{v}\\
\partial_{\bar{v}}\end{bmatrix},

we obtain that (
5.2
) can be written as an Hamiltonian system in the complex coordinates (
5.1
) as

\displaystyle V_{t}={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\nabla_{U}H_{\mathbb{C}}\left(V\right),\qquad V\triangleq\begin{bmatrix}v\\
\bar{v}\end{bmatrix},

(5.3)

where the
complex Poisson tensor
(cf. (
2.5
)) is defined as

{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\triangleq-{\rm i}{\bm{J}}=\begin{bmatrix}0&{\rm i}\\
-{\rm i}&0\end{bmatrix}.

(5.4)

Next we define the Fourier symbol

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)\triangleq\sqrt{\frac{\left|\xi\right|}{2\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)}}=\sqrt[4]{\frac{\left|\xi\right|}{2\left(\gamma\left(\left|\xi\right|^{2}-1\right)-{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\frac{{|\xi|}}{2}-1\right)\right)}}\quad\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\frac{1}{4}}_{0},

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)^{-1}=\sqrt[4]{\frac{2\left(\gamma\left({\xi}^{2}-1\right)-{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\frac{{|\xi|}}{2}-1\right)\right)}{{|\xi|}}}\quad\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{1}{4}}_{0}.

Then, we consider the symplectic matrix

{\bm{S}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}(D)\triangleq{\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({D}\right)&0\\
0&\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({D}\right)^{-1}\end{bmatrix}}\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad{\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({D}\right)\triangleq{\bm{S}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}(D)\circ\mathcal{C}.

(5.6)

Notice that

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)&\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)\\
-{\rm i}\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)^{-1}&{\rm i}\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)^{-1}\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\in\left(\tilde{\Gamma}^{1/4}_{0}\right)^{2\times 2},

(5.7)

\displaystyle{\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{-1}\left({\xi}\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)^{-1}&{\rm i}\ \mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)\\
\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)^{-1}&-{\rm i}\ \mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\xi}\right)\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle\in\left(\tilde{\Gamma}^{1/4}_{0}\right)^{2\times 2}.

We define the
complex coordinates

U
as

\displaystyle U\triangleq\begin{bmatrix}u\\
\bar{u}\end{bmatrix}\triangleq{\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{-1}\left({D}\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}=\mathcal{C}^{-1}\circ{\bm{S}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{-1}(D)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}.

(5.8)

Notation 5.1
.

Let
K\in\mathbb{N}
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
0\leqslant K^{\prime}\leqslant K
. We work on a time interval
I=[0,T]
for some fixed
T>0
to be determined. This latter is a priori implicit but will correspond to
c\varepsilon^{-(N+1)}.

Moreover, from now on we denote with

•

A_{\left[m;K^{\prime}\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)
any generic element in the space
\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, while
{\bm{A}}_{\left[m;K^{\prime}\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)
is a generic element of
\left(\Sigma\Gamma^{m}_{K,K^{\prime},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
such that
{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[m;K^{\prime}\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}
is lineary Hamiltonian up to homogeneity
N
(cf.
Definition

A.2
), whose explicit expression may vary from line to line. We use as well the simplified notation
A_{\left[m;0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq A_{\left[m\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)
and
{\bm{A}}_{\left[m;0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq{\bm{A}}_{\left[m\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)
;

•

R_{\left[K^{\prime}\right]}\left(U\right)
and
{\bm{R}}_{\left[K^{\prime}\right]}\left(U\right)
any generic element in the space
\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and
\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,K^{\prime},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
respectively, whose explicit expression may vary from line to line. We denote as well
R_{\left[0\right]}\left(U\right)\triangleq R\left(U\right)
and
{\bm{R}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U\right)\triangleq{\bm{R}}\left(U\right).

We prove the following:

Proposition 5.2

(Kelvin-Helmholtz equations in complex Hamiltonian coordinates)
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma>0
,
{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varrho\geqslant 0
, for any
K\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
s_{0}>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that if
\eta,\psi\in B^{K}_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
is a solution of

1.9
, then
U
defined in (
5.8
) solves the complex Hamiltonian system

\displaystyle U_{t}={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\ {\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\ \omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{1}\left(U;x,\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\ \left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}U+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right)U,

(5.9)

where

•

{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}
is defined in
Eq.

5.4
;

•

{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
is defined as

{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\left(U,x\right)\triangleq\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}+\frac{\mathtt{f}\left(\left({\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(D\right)U\right)_{1};x\right)}{2}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix},

(5.10)

where
\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)
is defined in

;

•

{\bm{A}}_{1}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\in\left(\Sigma\Gamma^{1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
is defined as

{\bm{A}}_{1}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq-V_{{\mathtt{b}}}\left({\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(D\right)U;x\right)\xi\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}+\frac{B_{\mathtt{b}}\left({\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\left|D\right|\right)U;x\right)}{2}\ \left|\xi\right|\begin{bmatrix}{\rm i}&0\\
0&-{\rm i}\end{bmatrix},

where

and

are functions in defined in

and

;

•

{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
is defined as

{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U,x\right)\triangleq A_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\ \begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma}}\left(\frac{B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)}{2}+\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{2}\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)-w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\right)\ \begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix},

(5.11)

where
w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)
is defined in
Eq.

4.3
;

•

{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)
and
{\bm{R}}\left(U\right)
are as in
Notation

4.4
.

Proof.

Step 1

(Diagonalization of the linear part of (
4.1
))
.

We can diagonalize the matrix defined in (

) as

{\bm{L}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)={\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right){\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right){\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{-1}\left(\xi\right),\qquad{\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\triangleq{\rm i}\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}\in\left(\tilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{0}\right)^{2\times 2},

(5.12)

where
\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{0}
are defined in
Eq.

2.20
.
Defining
U
as in (
5.8
)
where
\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}
solves (
2.14
),
the vector-valued complex function
U
solves the diagonal linear system

U_{t}={\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(D\right)U.

In particular thanks to the relation (
5.8
) combined with (
5.5
), which implies that
\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\left|D\right|\right)\left[1\right]=0
,
assure us that if
\left(\eta,\psi\right)\in H^{s+\frac{1}{4}}_{0}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}\right)\times\dot{H}^{s-\frac{1}{4}}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{R}\right)
then
u\in H^{s}_{0}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}\right)\simeq\dot{H}^{s}\left(\mathbb{T};\mathbb{C}\right)
for
s>0
.

Next, expanding (
5.5
) we have that

\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)=\frac{1}{\left(2\gamma\left|\xi\right|\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}}+\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\right)^{2}\frac{1}{\left(2\gamma\left|\xi\right|\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}}+\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}};-\frac{9}{4}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right),

(5.13)

with
\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}};-\frac{9}{4}}\left(\left|\xi\right|\right)\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{-\frac{9}{4}}_{0}
.

Notation 5.3
.

From now on we use the abbreviated notation
\mathsf{M}\triangleq{\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\left|D\right|\right)
defined in (
5.7
) and
\mathsf{m}\triangleq\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\left|D\right|\right)
defined in (

) for the sake of brevity.

Step 2

(Reformulation of
Eq.

4.1
in complex coordiantes)
.

Recall the matrix of Fourier symbols
{\bm{L}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}
in (

) and the matrix of symbols
{\bm{Q}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}
in (

); we define

\displaystyle{\bm{Q}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}};\geqslant 1}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\triangleq{\bm{Q}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)-{\bm{L}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)=\begin{bmatrix}0&0\\
\mathsf{q}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)&0\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle\in\Sigma\Gamma^{2}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(5.14)

where, from
Eqs.

5.20
and
4.2
,
\mathsf{q}\left(U;x,\xi\right)
is the real-valued symbol

\displaystyle\mathsf{q}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)=

\displaystyle\gamma\ \mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)\left(\left|\xi\right|^{2}-1\right)-w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\left(1+2\eta\right)}-1\right)

(5.15)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma\ \mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{2}-w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|+\mathsf{q}_{0}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right),

with

\mathsf{q}_{0}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\triangleq-\gamma\ \mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)+{\mathtt{b}}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{1+2\eta}-1\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

Then, by (
5.12
),

4.1
becomes

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}_{t}-\mathsf{M}{\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(D\right)\mathsf{M}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}

(5.16)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{Q}}_{\ \gamma,{\mathtt{b}};\geqslant 1}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)+{\bm{B}}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\left|\xi\right|-{\rm i}V_{{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)\ \textnormal{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ \xi+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(\eta,\psi;x,\xi\right)\right)}\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle+{\bm{R}}\left(\eta,\psi\right)\begin{bmatrix}\eta\\
\psi\end{bmatrix}.

Since

\psi\end{bmatrix}
we can derive the evolution equation for
U
multiplying (
5.16
) from the left for
\mathsf{M}^{-1}
obtaining

\displaystyle U_{t}-{\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(D\right)U

(5.17)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{M}^{-1}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{Q}}_{\ \gamma,{\mathtt{b}};\geqslant 1}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x,\xi\right)+{\bm{B}}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|-{\rm i}V_{{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\ \textnormal{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\ \xi+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x,\xi\right)\right)}\mathsf{M}U

\displaystyle+\mathsf{M}^{-1}{\bm{R}}\left(\mathsf{M}U\right)\mathsf{M}U.

Recall that
\mathsf{M}^{-1}
is explicitly defined in (
5.7
).
We have the following relation

\mathsf{M}^{-1}\begin{bmatrix}A_{1}&A_{2}\\
A_{3}&A_{4}\end{bmatrix}\mathsf{M}=\\
\frac{1}{2}\begin{bmatrix}\mathsf{m}^{-1}A_{1}\mathsf{m}+\mathsf{m}A_{4}\mathsf{m}^{-1}+{\rm i}\mathsf{m}A_{3}\mathsf{m}-{\rm i}\mathsf{m}^{-1}A_{2}\mathsf{m}^{-1}&\mathsf{m}^{-1}A_{1}\mathsf{m}-\mathsf{m}A_{4}\mathsf{m}^{-1}+{\rm i}\mathsf{m}A_{3}\mathsf{m}+{\rm i}\mathsf{m}^{-1}A_{2}\mathsf{m}^{-1}\\
\mathsf{m}^{-1}A_{1}\mathsf{m}-\mathsf{m}A_{4}\mathsf{m}^{-1}-{\rm i}\mathsf{m}A_{3}\mathsf{m}-{\rm i}\mathsf{m}^{-1}A_{2}\mathsf{m}^{-1}&\mathsf{m}^{-1}A_{1}\mathsf{m}+\mathsf{m}A_{4}\mathsf{m}^{-1}-{\rm i}\mathsf{m}A_{3}\mathsf{m}+{\rm i}\mathsf{m}^{-1}A_{2}\mathsf{m}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}.

(5.18)

Notice now that given

\displaystyle a\left(U;x,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{m_{a}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

\displaystyle\alpha\left(\xi\right)\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{m_{\alpha}}_{0},

\displaystyle\beta\left(\xi\right)\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{m_{\beta}}_{0},

applying
Proposition

3.15
we have that

\displaystyle\alpha\left(D\right){\OpBWPlain\left(a\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}\beta\left(D\right)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left(a\left(U;x,\xi\right)\alpha\left(\xi\right)\beta\left(\xi\right)+\frac{1}{2{\rm i}}\ \left(a\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)_{x}\left(\alpha_{\xi}\left(\xi\right)\beta\left(\xi\right)-\alpha\left(\xi\right)\beta_{\xi}\left(\xi\right)\right)\right)}

(5.19)

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left(\Sigma\Gamma^{m_{a}+m_{\alpha}+m_{\beta}-2}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)}+\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

Then, applying (

), we have that

\mathsf{M}^{-1}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{Q}}_{\ \gamma,{\mathtt{b}};\geqslant 1}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x,\xi\right)\right)}\mathsf{M}=\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\ \mathsf{m}\ {\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{q}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}\mathsf{m}\ \begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix},

(5.20)

where
\mathsf{q}\left(U;x,\xi\right)

We apply

Thus, combining

5.20

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}^{-1}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{Q}}_{\ \gamma,{\mathtt{b}};\geqslant 1}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x,\xi\right)\right)}\mathsf{M}

(5.22)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\ \left({\OpBWPlain\left(\mathsf{q}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left(\xi\right)+A_{\left[-\frac{1}{2}\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+R\left(U\right)\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}.

Next we apply

and

to the matrix-valued symbol

defined in (

) and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}^{-1}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{B}}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|\right)}\mathsf{M}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}{\OpBWPlain\left(B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|\right)}\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}+\frac{{\rm i}}{4}{\OpBWPlain\left(B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left(\xi\right)\right)}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle+{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right).

The same procedure give us the conjugations

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}^{-1}{\OpBWPlain\left({\rm i}V_{{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\xi\right)}\textnormal{Id}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathsf{M}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\OpBWPlain\left({\rm i}V_{{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\xi\right)}\textnormal{Id}_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}+{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right),

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathsf{M}^{-1}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x,\xi\right)\right)}\mathsf{M}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right),

while
Proposition

3.17
implies that

\mathsf{M}^{-1}{\bm{R}}\left(\mathsf{M}U\right)\mathsf{M}\leadsto{\bm{R}}\left(U\right).

(5.25)

We plug

,

,

and

and obtain that

U_{t}-{\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(D\right)U={\OpBWPlain\left(\sum_{\mathsf{j}=0}^{2}{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3-\mathsf{j}}{2}}^{\textnormal{NH}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}U+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right)U,

(5.26)

with

{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3-\mathsf{j}}{2}}^{\textnormal{NH}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\in\left(\Sigma\Gamma^{\frac{3-\mathsf{j}}{2}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2},

and are explicitly defined as

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}^{\textnormal{NH}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\ \mathsf{q}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left(\xi\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix},

(5.27)

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}_{1}^{\textnormal{NH}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle-{\rm i}V_{{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\xi\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}+\frac{B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)}{2}\ \left|\xi\right|\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\textnormal{NH}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}}{4}\ B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left(\xi\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}.

We now expand the symbols in (

) in decreasing para-differential orders using
Eqs.

5.15
and

, thus obtaining that

\displaystyle\mathsf{q}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left(\xi\right)=

(5.28)

\displaystyle B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left(\xi\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma}}\ B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+A_{\left[-\frac{1}{2}\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right).

Thus, inserting (
5.28
) in (
