), we obtain that

\sum_{\mathsf{j}=0}^{2}{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3-\mathsf{j}}{2}}^{\textnormal{NH}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)=\sum_{\mathsf{j}=0}^{2}{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3-\mathsf{j}}{2}}^{\textnormal{H}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{\left[-\frac{1}{2}\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right),

where

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}^{\textnormal{H}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\ \sqrt{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\ \mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{3}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}_{1}^{\textnormal{H}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{A}}_{1}^{\textnormal{NH}}\left(U;x,\xi\right),

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\textnormal{H}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma}}\left(\frac{B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)}{2}+\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\right)^{2}\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)-w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\ \begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}.

Therefore, (
5.26
) becomes

U_{t}-{\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(D\right)U={\OpBWPlain\left(\sum_{\mathsf{j}=0}^{2}{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3-\mathsf{j}}{2}}^{\textnormal{H}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}U+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right)U.

(5.29)

We now further refine the expression deduced in (
5.29
) by expressing the leading term as a quasilinear perturbation of the unperturbed frequencies
\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)
, cf. (
2.20
). Using (
2.21
) we have that

{\bm{A}}^{\textnormal{H}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\left(U;x,\xi\right)={\rm i}\ \left(\frac{\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)}{2}\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma}}\frac{\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)}{2}\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\right)^{2}\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[-\frac{1}{2}\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix},

so that, defining

\displaystyle{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2};\geqslant 1}\left(U,x\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\ \frac{\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)}{2}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}\ {\rm i}\ ,

\displaystyle{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U,x\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma}}\left(\frac{B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)}{2}+2\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\right)^{2}\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)-w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\right)\ \begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}\ {\rm i},

\displaystyle{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{1}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}^{\textnormal{H}}_{1}\left(U;x,\xi\right),

we transform (
5.29
) into

\displaystyle U_{t}-{\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(D\right)U

(5.30)

\displaystyle={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\ {\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2};\geqslant 1}\left(U;x\right)\ \omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{1}\left(U;x,\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\ \left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}U+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right)U.

Next, an explicit computation shows that

{\bm{D}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)=\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\ {\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}.

Thus, defining

\displaystyle{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\triangleq\begin{bmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}+{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2};\geqslant 1}\left(U,x\right),

it remains only to show that (
5.30
) is a complex Hamiltonian system and that
{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)
is linearly symplectic. Indeed complex variables transformation
{\bm{\mathsf{M}}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{-1}
in (
5.8
) is the composition of the complex transformation
{\mathcal{C}}^{-1}
in (
5.1
) and the real symplectic map
{\bm{S}}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{-1}(D)
, see (
5.6
). Then the equation for
U
has the complex Hamiltonian form in (
5.3
). Then we apply Lemma
A.14
to each homogeneous component in (
5.30
). As the explicit positive order symbols are linearly symplectic by direct inspections, we eventually replace
{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)
as in (
A.18
) to get a linearly symplectic matrix of symbols. This conclude the proof.

∎

6
Block diagonalization and reduction to constant coefficients

In this section, we perform spectrally localized, linearly symplectic, and bounded transformations to conjugate the complex Kelvin-Helmholtz system (
5.9
) into a diagonal constant-coefficient system, up to a smoothing remainder. Specifically, we prove the following.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varrho>3\left(N+1\right)
, there exists
\underline{K^{\prime}}>0
such that for any
K>\underline{K^{\prime}}
there are
s_{0}>0
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that, for any solution
U\in B^{K}_{s_{0}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
solution of (
5.9
) there exists a real-to-real invertible matrix of spectrally localized maps
{\bm{B}}\left(U;t\right)
such that

1.

{\bm{B}}\left(U;t\right),\ {\bm{B}}\left(U;t\right)^{-1}\in\left({\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}}-1,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
are linearly symplectic up to homogeneity
N
, according to
Definition

A.4
. Moreover,
{\bm{B}}\left(U;t\right)-\textnormal{Id}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{3}{2}\left(N+1\right)}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}}-1,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2};

2.

The variable
W\triangleq{\bm{B}}\left(U;t\right)U
solves

W_{t}=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\ \left[\left(1+\mathpzc{v}\left(U;t\right)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)+\mathpzc{V}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(U;t\right)\xi+\mathpzc{b}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;t\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathpzc{b}_{0}\left(U;t,\xi\right)\right]\right)W+\boldsymbol{\mathsf{R}}\left(U;t\right)W,

(6.1)

where

•

\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\in\tilde{\Gamma}^{\frac{3}{2}}_{0}
is defined in (
2.20
);

•

\mathpzc{v}\left(U;t\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and is independent of
x
;

•

\mathpzc{V}_{\mathtt{b}}\left(U;t\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,1,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and is independent of
x
;

•

\mathpzc{b}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;t\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,2,2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
and is independent of
x
;

•

\mathpzc{b}_{0}\left(U;t,\xi\right)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},2}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
, is independent of
x
and such that
\textnormal{Im}\mathpzc{b}_{0}\left(U;t,\xi\right)\in\Gamma^{0}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},N+1}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right]
;

•

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{R}}\left(U;t\right)\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho+3\left(N+1\right)}_{K,\underline{K^{\prime}},1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
.

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of
Proposition

6.1
. The procedure is now rather classical so we state the main steps, for the missing details, we refer the interested reader to
[
27
, Section 6]
.

6.1
A complex Alinhac Good Unknown

We first perform a change of variable, known as
Alinhac good unknown
that cancels the anti-diagonal terms of order one in (
5.9
).

Lemma 6.2

(Alinhac Good Unknown for the Kelvin-Helmholtz equations in complex coordinates)
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma>0
,
{\mathtt{b}}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varrho\geqslant 0
, for any
K\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
s_{0}>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that if
U\in B^{K}_{s_{0}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
solves (
5.9
) there exists a real-to-real matrix of operators
\mathbf{G}(U)
such that

1.

{\bm{G}}\left(U\right),\ {\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}\in\left({\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
are linearly symplectic, according to
Definition

A.3
. Moreover,
{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)-\textnormal{Id}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
;

2.

The variable
U_{0}\triangleq{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)U
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}U_{0}

\displaystyle={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\ {\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}^{(0)}\left(U;x\right)\ \omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(0)}\left(U;x\right)\ \left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}U_{0}

(6.2)

\displaystyle-\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\xi\right)U_{0}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right)U_{0},

where

•

the symbols
{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}^{(0)}\triangleq{\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}},\ \omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}
and
V_{\mathtt{b}}
are the same as in the statement of
Proposition

5.2
and
{\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}
,
{\bm{R}}
are as in
Notation

5.1
;

•

{\bm{A}}^{(0)}_{\frac{1}{2}}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
is defined as

{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(U;x\right)\triangleq A_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\left(U;x\right)\ \begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}\triangleq\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma}}\left(\frac{{\mathtt{b}}^{2}}{2}\mathtt{f}\left(\eta;x\right)-w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\right)\ \begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix},

where
w_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\eta,\psi;x\right)
is defined in
Eq.

4.3
.

Proof.

The procedure that we use here is the same as the one proved in
[
27
, Section 6.1]
, thus we omit to perform detailed computations, in particular the
Item

1
is true by direct computations and the fact that
{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)-\textnormal{Id}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
. This latter fact is a consequence of
Lemma

3.7
. The only difference, compared to
[
27
]
, is the symbol
{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\in\Sigma\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
appearing in (
5.9
) and defined explicitly in (
5.11
), which shall be treated in detail. Let us define the transformation

{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)\triangleq\textnormal{Id}-\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}{\OpBWPlain\left(B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left({\xi}\right)\right)}.

(6.3)

The fact that
{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)
is a nilpotent perturbation of the identity allows us to compute its inverse as

{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}\triangleq\textnormal{Id}+\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}{\OpBWPlain\left(B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left({\xi}\right)\right)}.

Thus, defining

U_{0}\triangleq{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)U
,
and using the conjugation rules

\displaystyle{\bm{G}}\left(U\right){\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\right)}{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)-{\rm i}\frac{B_{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\left|\xi\right|\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}+\frac{B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}}{4}\ \mathsf{m}^{2}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right),

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)\OpvecPlain\left(-{\rm i}V_{\mathtt{b}}\xi\right)\textnormal{Id}_{\mathbb{C}^{2}}{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}=

\displaystyle\OpvecPlain\left(-{\rm i}V_{\mathtt{b}}\xi\right)\textnormal{Id}_{\mathbb{C}^{2}},

\displaystyle{\bm{G}}\left(U\right){\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\rm i}\frac{B_{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\left|\xi\right|\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}\right)}{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\rm i}\frac{B_{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\left|\xi\right|\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}-\frac{B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}}{2}\ \mathsf{m}^{2}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right),

we get, using also
Eqs.

6.4
and
5.13
,

\displaystyle{\bm{G}}\left(U\right){\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{1}\right)}{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma}}\frac{B^{2}_{\mathtt{b}}}{4}\ \left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0\right]}\right)}

(6.5)

\displaystyle+\OpvecPlain\left(-{\rm i}V_{\mathtt{b}}\xi\right)+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right).

Due to the particular nilpotent structure of
{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}
and
{\bm{G}(U)}-\textnormal{Id}
,
the conjugation for the symbol of order
1/2
explicitly given by

{\bm{G}}\left(U\right){\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}.

(6.6)

Remark that there is a cancellation of the terms of the form
|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}
between (
5.11
) and (
6.5
) leading the the expression of
{\bm{A}}^{(0)}_{\frac{1}{2}}
in the statement. Moreover, we have

{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)U_{t}=\partial_{t}U_{0}-\left(\partial_{t}{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)\right){\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{0}.

(6.7)

Hence, from
Items

1
and
3.15
, we have that

\left(\partial_{t}{\bm{G}}\left(U\right)\right){\bm{G}}\left(U\right)^{-1}={\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[-\frac{1}{2};1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)},\quad{\bm{A}}_{\left[-\frac{1}{2};1\right]}\triangleq-\frac{{\rm i}}{2}\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}{\partial_{t}B_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\mathsf{m}_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}^{2}\left({\xi}\right)}.

(6.8)

The conjugations proved in
Eqs.

6.5
,
6.6
,
6.7
and
6.8
and the computations performed in
[
27
, Section 6.1]
conclude thus the proof of
Lemma

6.2
. We remark that the zero-th order matrix
{\bm{A}}_{[0,1]}
is the sum of the linearly Hamiltonian matrix
{\bm{A}}_{[-\frac{1}{2},1]}
and the contributions coming from
{\bm{A}}_{[0]}
in
Eqs.

6.4
and
6.5
. To prove that
{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{[0]}
is linearly Hamiltonian up to homogeneity
N
we then apply Lemma
A.15
to each homogeneous component of the operators in
Eqs.

6.4
and
6.5
which are linearly Hamiltonian thanks to
Lemma

A.5
.
∎

6.2
Diagonalization at the highest order

In the present section, we diagonalize the quasi-linear contribution

{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}^{(0)}_{\frac{3}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\right)},

which reduces to the diagonalization of the matrix

{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}^{(0)}_{\frac{3}{2}}\left(U;x\right)={\rm i}\begin{bmatrix}-\left(1+\mathtt{f}\left(U;x\right)\right)&-\mathtt{f}\left(U;x\right)\\
\mathtt{f}\left(U;x\right)&1+\mathtt{f}\left(U;x\right)\end{bmatrix},

(6.9)

where
\mathtt{f}\left(U;x\right)
is defined in (
4.72
).
The eigenvalues of (
6.9
) are given by
\pm{\rm i}\ \lambda\left(U;x\right)
where

\lambda\left(U;x\right)\triangleq\sqrt{1+2\mathtt{f}\left(U;x\right)}

(6.10)

and we have that
\lambda\left(U;x\right)-1\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
. Defining

\displaystyle\mathtt{h}\triangleq\frac{1+\mathtt{f}+\lambda}{\sqrt{\left(1+\mathtt{f}+\lambda\right)^{2}-\mathtt{f}^{2}}}\ ,

\displaystyle\mathtt{g}\triangleq\frac{-\mathtt{f}}{\sqrt{\left(1+\mathtt{f}+\lambda\right)^{2}-\mathtt{f}^{2}}},

the symmetric and symplectic matrix

\displaystyle{\bm{F}}\triangleq\begin{bmatrix}\mathtt{h}&\mathtt{g}\\
\mathtt{g}&\mathtt{h}\end{bmatrix}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}\ ,

\displaystyle{\bm{F}}^{-1}\triangleq\begin{bmatrix}\mathtt{h}&-\mathtt{g}\\
-\mathtt{g}&\mathtt{h}\end{bmatrix}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}\ ,

(6.11)

which are such that

{\bm{F}}-\textnormal{Id},\ {\bm{F}}^{-1}-\textnormal{Id}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}\ ,

(6.12)

diagonalize (
6.9
) in the sense that

{\bm{F}}^{-1}{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}^{(0)}_{\frac{3}{2}}{\bm{F}}=\begin{bmatrix}-{\rm i}\lambda&0\\
0&{\rm i}\lambda\end{bmatrix}.

We prove the following result.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varrho>0
, for any
K\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
there are
s_{0}>0
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that, for any solution
U\in B^{K}_{s_{0}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
of (
6.2
) there exists a real-to-real invertible matrix of spectrally localized maps
{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}(U)
such that

1.

{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}(U),\ {\bm{\Psi}}_{1}(U)^{-1}\in\left({\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,0,0}\left[\epsilon_{0}\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
are linearly symplectic as per
Definition

A.3
. Moreover,
{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}(U)-\textnormal{Id}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}.

2.

The variable
U_{1}\triangleq{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}(U)U_{0}
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}U_{1}

\displaystyle=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\ \lambda\left(U;x\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)-{\rm i}\ V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(1)}\left(U;x\right)\xi\right)U_{1}

(6.13)

\displaystyle+{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}U_{1}+{\bm{R}}_{\left[1\right]}\left(U\right)U_{1},

where

•

\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\in\Gamma^{\frac{3}{2}}_{0}
is defined in (
2.20
);

•

\lambda\left(U;x\right)
is defined in (
6.10
);

•

V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(1)}\left(U;x\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
;

•

{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(1)}\left(U;x\right)\triangleq\left(\mathtt{h}\left(U;x\right)+\mathtt{g}\left(U;x\right)\right)^{2}{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(0)}\left(U;x\right)\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
.

Proof.

Arguing as in
[
27
, Lemma 6.4]
the 1-flow
{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)\triangleq\left.{\bm{\Psi}}^{\tau}\left(U\right)\right|_{\tau=0}
of

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}{\bm{\Psi}}^{\tau}\left(U\right)={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\begin{bmatrix}{\rm i}\ \log\left(\mathtt{h}\left(U;x\right)+\mathtt{g}\left(U;x\right)\right)&0\\
0&-{\rm i}\ \log\left(\mathtt{h}\left(U;x\right)+\mathtt{g}\left(U;x\right)\right)\end{bmatrix}\right)}{\bm{\Psi}}^{\tau},\\
&{\bm{\Psi}}^{0}\left(U\right)=\textnormal{Id}\end{aligned}\right.

(6.14)

is such that

\displaystyle{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)={\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{F}}^{-1}\left(U;x\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right),

\displaystyle{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}={\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{F}}\left(U;x\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right).

(6.15)

Since the generator in (
6.14
) is linearly Hamiltonian,
Lemma

A.6
ensures that
{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)
is a linearly symplectic, spectrally localized map in
\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},1}[r,N]\right)^{2\times 2}
.
Thus, the new variable
U_{1}\triangleq{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)U_{0}
solves (cf. (
6.2
))

\partial_{t}U_{1}={\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right){\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\ {\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}^{(0)}\left(U;x\right)\ \omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)+{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(0)}\left(U;x\right)\ \left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{1}\\
+\partial_{t}{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right){\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{1}-{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right){\OpBWPlain\left({\rm i}V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(\mathsf{M}U;x\right)\xi\right)}{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{1}+{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right){\bm{R}}\left(U\right){\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{1}.

(6.16)

Following the same computations as in
[
27
, Eq. (6.3)]
we have that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right){\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\ {\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{3}{2}}^{(0)}\left(U;x\right)\ \omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\right)}{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}

(6.17)

\displaystyle={\OpBWPlain\left({\rm i}\lambda\left(U;x\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\begin{bmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}+A_{\left[-\frac{1}{2};0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right).

Similar computations give us the conjugation

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right){\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\ {\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(0)}\left(U;x\right)\ \left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}

(6.18)

\displaystyle={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left(\left(\mathtt{h}\left(U;x\right)+\mathtt{g}\left(U;x\right)\right)^{2}{\bm{A}}^{(0)}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\ \left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+A_{\left[-\frac{1}{2};0\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right)

and

\displaystyle{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right){\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right),

(6.19)

while from
Eqs.

6.15
and
6.12
we obtain that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right){\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}={\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}_{\left[1\right]}\left(U\right),

(6.20)

and standard symbolic computations (cf.
Propositions

3.15
and
6.15
) give that

\displaystyle{\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}V_{\mathtt{b}}\left(U;x\right)\xi\right){\bm{\Psi}}_{1}\left(U\right)^{-1}=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(1)}\left(U;x\right)\xi\right)+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right),

\displaystyle V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(1)}\left(U;x\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

(6.21)

We conclude plugging
Eqs.

6.17
,
6.18
,
6.19
,
6.20
and
6.21
in
Eq.

6.16
. We remark that the zero-th order matrix
{\bm{A}}_{[0,1]}
is the sum of
{\bm{A}}_{[-\frac{1}{2};0]}
form
Eqs.

6.17
and
6.18
and
{\bm{A}}_{[0;1]}
from
Eqs.

6.19
and
6.20
. To prove that
{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{[0;1]}
is linearly symplectic we then apply Lemma
A.15
to each homogeneous components of the spectrally localized operators in
Eqs.

6.17
,
6.18
,
6.19
and
6.20
, which are linearly Hamiltonian thanks to
Lemma

A.5
.
∎

6.3
Reduction to constant coefficients at the highest order

Lemma 6.4

(Reduction of the highest order)
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varrho>2(N+1)
. Then for any
K\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
there are
s_{0}>0
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for any solution
U\in B^{K}_{s_{0}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
of (
6.13
), there exists a real-to-real invertible matrix of spectrally localized maps
{\bm{\Psi}}_{2}(U)
satisfying

1.

{\bm{\Psi}}_{2}(U),\,{\bm{\Psi}}_{2}(U)\in\left({\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,0,0}[\epsilon_{0}]\right)^{2\times 2}
are linearly symplectic as per
Definition

A.3
. Moreover,

{\bm{\Psi}}_{2}(U)-\textnormal{Id}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{N+1}_{K,0,2}[\epsilon_{0},N]\right)^{2\times 2}
;

2.

The variable
U_{2}\triangleq{\bm{\Psi}}_{2}(U)U_{1}
solves the system

\displaystyle\partial_{t}U_{2}

\displaystyle=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\ \left[\left(1+\mathpzc{v}(U)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)-V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(2)}(U;x)\xi\right]\right)U_{2}

(6.22)

\displaystyle+{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}(U;x,\xi)\right)}U_{2}+{\bf R}(U)U_{2},

where

•

\mathpzc{v}(U)
is a
x
-independent function in
\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,2}[\epsilon_{0},N]
and
\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}(\xi)
is defined in (
2.20
);

•

V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(2)}(U;x)
is a real valued function in
\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,1,1}[\epsilon_{0},N]\right)^{2}
;

•

{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}\left(U;x\right)\triangleq A_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}\left(U;x\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
1&1\end{bmatrix}
where
A_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}\left(U;x\right)\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]\right)^{2\times 2}
;

•

\mathbf{R}\left(U\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho+2\left(N+1\right)}_{K,1,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

Proof.

We refer the interested reader to
[
27
, Lemma 6.7]
, the only difference being the conjugation of the term of order
1/2
, which is achieved by standard paracomposition theorems, such as
[
20
, Theorem 3.27]
. Notice that the conjugation worsens the regularizing properties of the smoothing operator in
Eq.

6.22
.
∎

6.4
Diagonalization up to smoothing remainders

In this section we block-diagonalize the Kelvin-Helmholtz system up to a smoothing remainder.

Lemma 6.5

(Diagonalization to arbitrary order)
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varrho\gg N
. Then for any
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\left\{-1\right\}
, there exists
K^{\prime}\triangleq K^{\prime}(n)\geqslant 0
such that for all
K\geqslant K^{\prime}+1
, there exist
s_{0}>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for any solution
U\in B^{K}_{s_{0},{\mathbb{R}}}\left(I;\epsilon_{0}\right)
of
Eq.

6.22
, there exists a real-to-real invertible matrix of spectrally localized maps
{\bm{\Phi}}_{n}(U)
satisfying

1.

{\bm{\Phi}}_{n}(U),\,{\bm{\Phi}}_{n}(U)^{-1}\in\left({\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,0,0}[\epsilon_{0}]\right)^{2\times 2}
are linearly symplectic up to homogeneity
N
as per
Definition

A.4
. Moreover,

{\bm{\Phi}}_{n}(U)-\textnormal{Id}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},1}[r,N]\right)^{2\times 2}
;

2.

The variable
U_{n+3}\triangleq{\bm{\Phi}}_{n}(U)U_{2}
solves

\displaystyle\partial_{t}U_{n+4}

\displaystyle=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\ \left[\left(1+\mathpzc{v}(U)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)-V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(2)}(U;x)\xi+a^{\left(n\right)}_{\left.1\middle/2\right.}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+a^{(n)}_{0}(U;x,\xi)\right]\right)U_{n+3}

(6.23)

\displaystyle+{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[-n;K^{\prime}+1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}U_{n+3}+{\bf R}\left(U\right)U_{n+3},

where

•

a^{\left(n\right)}_{\frac{1}{2}}\left(U;x\right)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
,

•

a^{(n)}_{0}(U;x,\xi)
is a symbol in
\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},1}[\epsilon_{0},N]
with
\textnormal{Im}a^{(n)}_{0}(U;x,\xi)\in\Gamma^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},N+1}[\epsilon_{0}]
,

•

\mathbf{R}\left(U\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho+2\left(N+1\right)}_{K,1,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]
.

Proof.

Step 1

(Reduction of the term of order
1/2
)
.

Notice that the term of order
1/2
can be written as

\displaystyle{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\left.1\middle/2\right.}=-{\rm i}a_{\left.1\middle/2\right.}^{(-1)}\left(U;x\right)\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
-1&-1\end{bmatrix}\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle a_{\left.1\middle/2\right.}^{(-1)}\left(U;x\right)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right].

Let now

\displaystyle{\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq\begin{bmatrix}0&F^{\left(-1\right)}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\\
F^{\left(-1\right)}\left(U;x,\xi\right)&0\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle F^{\left(-1\right)}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\triangleq\frac{a_{\left.1\middle/2\right.}^{(-1)}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\left.1\middle/2\right.}}{2\left(1+\mathpzc{v}\left(U\right)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)}\in\Sigma\Gamma^{-1}_{K,0,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right]

and let
\left({\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}}^{\tau}\left(U\right)\right)_{\left|\tau\right|\leqslant 1}
be the flow generated by
{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}
with initial condition
{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}}^{0}\left(U\right)=\textnormal{Id}
as per
Lemma

A.6
. We denote with
{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)\triangleq{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}}^{1}\left(U\right)
. Since
\bm{F}^{(-1)}
is linearly Hamiltonian,
Lemma

A.6
ensures that
{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)
is a linearly symplectic, spectrally localized map in
\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},1}[r,N]\right)^{2\times 2}
.
Let us define

U_{3}\triangleq{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}}^{1}\left(U\right)U_{2}={\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)U_{2},

which is the solution of

\displaystyle\partial_{t}U_{3}

\displaystyle={\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\ \left[\left(1+\mathpzc{v}(U)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)-V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(2)}(U;x)\xi\right]\right){\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{3}

(6.24)

\displaystyle+\left(\partial_{t}{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)\right){\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{3}+{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right){\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}(U;x,\xi)\right)}{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{3}

\displaystyle+{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right){\bf R}(U){\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)^{-1}U_{3}.

The following conjugation rule apply (cf.
[
21
, Lemma A.1]
) setting
{\bf F}\triangleq{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}

\displaystyle{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right){\bm{M}}\left(U\right){\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)^{-1}=

\displaystyle\ {\bm{M}}+\sum_{q=1}^{L}\frac{1}{q!}\textnormal{Ad}^{q}_{\bf F}\left[\bm{M}\right]+\frac{1}{L!}\int_{0}^{1}\left(1-\tau\right)^{L}{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}}^{\tau}\left(U\right)\textnormal{Ad}^{L+1}_{\bf F}\left[\bm{M}\right]{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{\left(-1\right)}}^{\tau}\left(U\right)^{-1}\textnormal{d}\tau,

(6.25)

\displaystyle\left(\partial_{t}{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)\right){\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)^{-1}=

\displaystyle\ {\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[-1;1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}_{\left[1\right]}\left(U\right),

(6.26)

An application of
Eq.

6.25
for
L>\frac{3+2\varrho}{4}-1
combined with symbolic calculus considerations give us that

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)\left(\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\ \left(1+\mathpzc{v}(U)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)\right)+{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\frac{1}{2}}^{(2)}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+{\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}(U;x,\xi)\right)}\right){\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)^{-1}

(6.27)

\displaystyle=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\left[\left(1+\mathpzc{v}(U)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)+a_{\left.1\middle/2\right.}^{(-1)}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)+{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}\left(U;x,\xi\right)\right)}+{\bm{R}}\left(U\right),

in which the off-diagonal terms of order 1/2 have been canceled.
Applying
Eqs.

6.27
,
6.25
and
6.26
to (
6.24
) (and renaming
{\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right){\bf R}(U){\bm{\Phi}}\left(U\right)^{-1}\leadsto{\bf R}(U)
in light of
Proposition

3.17
,
Item

2
we obtain that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}U_{3}

\displaystyle=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\ \left[\left(1+\mathpzc{v}(U)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)-V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(2)}(U;x)\xi+a_{\left.1\middle/2\right.}^{(2)}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]\right)U_{3}

(6.28)

\displaystyle+{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[0;1\right]}(U;x,\xi)\right)}U_{3}+{\bm{R}}(U)U_{3}.

Notice that the conjugation rule in
Eq.

6.25
does not modify the principal symbol of the transport term. Moreover the zero-th order matrix
{\bm{A}}_{[0,1]}
is the sum of
{\bm{A}}_{[-1;1]}
form
Eq.

6.26
and
{\bm{A}}_{[0;1]}
from
Eq.

6.27
. To prove that
{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{[0;1]}
is linearly symplectic we then apply Lemma
A.15
to each homogeneous components of the spectrally localized operators in
Eqs.

6.26
and
6.27
, which are linearly Hamiltonian thanks to
Lemma

A.5
.

Step 2

(Reduction at non-positive order)
.

We sketch the proof which is very similar to the one outlined in
Step

1
, we refer the interested reader to
[
27
, Lemma 6.8]
. The proof is performed by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the case
n=0
is proven in
Step

1
, so that we can consider the case
n\leadsto n+1
. Suppose
Eq.

6.23
holds, we have to find a bounded, lineary-symplectic transformation that pushes the off diagonal terms of
{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[-n;K^{\prime}+1\right]}\right)}
to lower order, similarly as in was done in
Step

1
. Since the matrix
{\bm{A}}_{\left[-n;K^{\prime}+1\right]}
is of the form

\displaystyle{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\bm{A}}_{\left[-n;K^{\prime}+1\right]}={\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}\begin{bmatrix}-{\rm i}\bar{b}^{\vee}_{\left[-n\right]}&-\bar{a}^{\vee}_{\left[-n\right]}\\
-{a}_{\left[-n\right]}&{\rm i}\bar{b}_{\left[-n\right]}\end{bmatrix},

\displaystyle{a}_{\left[-n\right]},\ \bar{b}_{\left[-n\right]}\in\Sigma\Gamma^{-n}_{K,K^{\prime}+1,1}\left[\epsilon_{0},N\right],

(6.29)

such cancellation is achieved via conjugation by the flow

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{(n)}}^{\tau}(U)={\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{F}}^{(n)}(U)\right)}{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{(n)}}^{\tau}(U),\\
&{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{(n)}}^{0}(U)=\textnormal{Id},\end{aligned}\right.

\displaystyle{\bm{F}}^{(n)}(U)\triangleq\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}0&f_{-n-\frac{3}{2}}\\
\overline{f_{-n-\frac{3}{2}}^{\vee}}&0\end{array}\right],

f_{-n-\frac{3}{2}}(U;t,x,\xi)\triangleq-\frac{b_{-n}(U;x,\xi)}{2{\rm i}\omega(\xi)(1+\mathpzc{v}(U))}\in\Sigma\Gamma_{K,K^{\prime}+1,1}^{-n-\frac{3}{2}}[\epsilon_{0},N],

and defining the variable
U_{n+4}\triangleq{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{\left(n\right)}}^{1}\left(U\right)U_{n+3}

and we refer the reader to
[
27
, Lemma 6.8]
for further details. As the matrix in (
6.29
) is linearly Hamiltonian up to homogeneity
N
by inductive hypothesis, the explicitly defined generator
\bm{F}^{(n)}
is linearly Hamiltonian up to homogeneity
N
as well. Thus
Lemma

A.6
ensures that
{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{(n)}}^{\tau}(U)
is a linearly symplectic, spectrally localized map in
\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{0}_{K,K^{\prime},1}[r,N]\right)^{2\times 2}
. The bounded, linearly symplectic transformation is thus defined as

{\bm{\Phi}}_{n}\left(U\right)\triangleq\prod_{\mathsf{j}=-1}^{n}{\bm{\Phi}}_{{\bm{F}}^{\left(\mathsf{j}\right)}}^{1}\left(U\right).

∎

We can thus apply
Lemma

6.5
setting
n\triangleq n_{1}\left(\varrho\right)\geqslant-\varrho+2\left(N+1\right)
so that
{\bm{J}}_{\mathbb{C}}{\OpBWPlain\left({\bm{A}}_{\left[-n;K^{\prime}+1\right]}\right)}
can be incorporated in the smoothing reminder
{\bf R}\left(U\right)
, thus setting
Z\triangleq U_{n_{1}+4}
we obtain that
Z
solves the evolution equation

\partial_{t}Z=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\ \left[\left(1+\mathpzc{v}(U)\right)\omega_{\gamma,{\mathtt{b}}}\left(\xi\right)-V_{\mathtt{b}}^{(2)}(U;x)\xi+a^{\left(n_{1}\right)}_{\left.1\middle/2\right.}\left(U;x\right)\left|\xi\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}+a^{(n_{1})}_{0}(U;x,\xi)\right]\right)Z+{\bf R}\left(U\right)Z.

(6.30)

6.5
Reduction to constant coefficients up to smoothing remainders

Notation 6.6
.

From now on we shall denote functions and symbols that are
x
-independent with calligraphic lower- and upper-case letters.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varrho>3(N+1)
.
Then for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
there is
K^{\prime\prime}\triangleq K^{\prime\prime}\left(\varrho,n\right)>0
such that for all
K\geqslant K^{\prime\prime}+1
there are
s_{0}>0
,
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for any solution

U\in B_{s_{0},\mathbb{R}}^{K}(I;\epsilon_{0})
of (
5.9
), there exists a real-to-real invertible matrix of spectrally localized maps
{\bm{\Theta}}_{n}(U)
such that

1.

{\bm{\Theta}}_{n}(U),{\bm{\Theta}}_{n}(U)^{-1}\in\left({\mathcal{S}}_{K,K^{\prime\prime},0}^{0}[\epsilon_{0}]\right)^{2\times 2}
are linearly symplectic up to homogeneity
N
according to Definition
A.4
. Moreover,
{\bm{\Theta}}_{n}(U)-\textnormal{Id}\in\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{N+1}{2}}_{K,K^{\prime\prime},1}[\epsilon_{0},N]\right)^{2\times 2}.

2.

If
Z
solves (
6.30
) then the variable

Z_{n}\triangleq{\bm{\Theta}}_{n}(U)Z
solves

\partial_{t}Z_{n}=\OpvecPlain\left({\rm i}\,\mathpzc{d}_{\left.3\middle/2\right.}^{(n)}(U;t,\xi)+{\rm i}a_{-\frac{n}{2}}(U;t,x,\xi)\right)Z_{n}+\boldsymbol{\mathsf{R}}(U;t)Z_{n},

(6.31)

with the
x
–independent symbol

\mathpzc{d}_{3/2}^{(n)}(U;t,\xi)\triangleq(1+\mathpzc{v}(U;t))\omega(\xi)+\mathpzc{V}_{\mathtt{b}}(U;t)\xi+\mathpzc{b}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U;t)|\xi|^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathpzc{b}_{0}^{(n)}(U;t,\xi),

(6.32)

where

•

\mathpzc{v}(U)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,0,2}[\epsilon_{0},N]
;

•

the function
\mathpzc{V}(U;t)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,1,2}[\epsilon_{0},N]
is

x
-independent;

•

the function
\mathpzc{b}_{\frac{1}{2}}(U;t)\in\Sigma{\mathcal{F}}^{\mathbb{R}}_{K,2,2}[\epsilon_{0},N]
is

x
-independent;

•

the symbol
\mathpzc{b}_{0}^{(n)}(U;t,\xi)\in\Sigma\Gamma^{0}_{K,K^{\prime\prime},2}[\epsilon_{0},N]

is
x
–independent and its imaginary part

is in

;

•

the symbol
a_{-\frac{n}{2}}(U;t,x,\xi)
belongs to
\Sigma\Gamma^{-\frac{n}{2}}_{K,K^{\prime\prime}+1,1}[\epsilon_{0},N]
and its imaginary part

is in
\Gamma^{-\frac{n}{2}}_{K,K^{\prime\prime}+1,N+1}[\epsilon_{0}]
;

•

\boldsymbol{\mathsf{R}}(U;t)
is a real-to-real matrix of smoothing operators in

\left(\Sigma{\mathcal{R}}^{-\varrho+3(N+1)}_{K,K^{\prime\prime}+1,1}[\epsilon_{0},N]\right)^{2\times 2}
.

Proof.

The proof consist of a minor modification of the proof of
[

, lemma 6.9]
, so we refer the interested reader to
[
